- [ ] **Step 8: Create `ExpectHooks` and route `VOICE_EXPECT` to it**

Create `app/src/debug/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/debug/ExpectHooks.kt`:

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.app.debug

import android.content.Intent
import android.os.Build
import android.os.SystemClock
import dev.kestrel.app.AppGraph
import dev.kestrel.app.debug.TestHooks.HookError
import dev.kestrel.app.listen.BoostWordBudget
import dev.kestrel.app.orchestrator.Event
import dev.kestrel.commands.Command
import dev.kestrel.commands.CommandParser
import dev.kestrel.commands.Commands

/**
 * Hook H2 of plan 4D, voice-expect: the phone compares the next transcripts of Kestrel's voice session with the text of a
 * clip that the PC plays, so that an air check reads the result of its own clip and never of a stray utterance
 * (2026-11-20-a71-checks-4d-interfaces.md, section 4). The match runs with its tag, as a tagged `voice -Send heard` does, or with `noRun`
 * is only resolved; every other transcript while the expectation holds is dropped. The answers hold counts, times on the
 * phone's clock and kinds, never what Kestrel heard.
 */
internal class ExpectHooks(private val graph: AppGraph) {
    fun run(command: String, intent: Intent): String = when (command) {
        "VOICE_EXPECT" -> if (intent.getBooleanExtra(EXTRA_RESULT, false)) result(tag(intent)) else expect(intent)
        else -> throw HookError("unknown command '$command'")
    }

    private fun tag(intent: Intent): String = intent.getStringExtra(EXTRA_TAG) ?: throw HookError("add --es tag <tag>")

    private fun expect(intent: Intent): String {
        val tag = tag(intent)
        if (!TAG.matches(tag)) throw HookError("add --es tag with 1 to 32 letters, digits, '-' and '_'")
        val text = intent.getStringExtra(EXTRA_TEXT) ?: throw HookError("add -Text with words")
        val seconds = intent.getIntExtra(EXTRA_SECONDS, DEFAULT_SECONDS)
        if (seconds !in 1..MAX_SECONDS) throw HookError("add --ei seconds with 1 to $MAX_SECONDS")
        val noRun = intent.getBooleanExtra(EXTRA_NO_RUN, false)
        if (!noRun && VoiceHooks.refused(text, Build.HARDWARE)) throw HookError(VoiceHooks.YES_CALL_REFUSED)
        val listening = graph.listening
        if (!listening.sessionOpen.value) throw HookError("Kestrel's voice session is not open")
        val now = SystemClock.elapsedRealtime()
        synchronized(lock) {
            val current = expectation
            if (current != null && current.waiting(now)) {
                throw HookError("an expectation waits; ask for its answer with -Result -Tag ${current.tag}")
            }
            if (current?.tag == tag || VoiceHooks.tags.tagged(tag) != null) {
                throw HookError("the tag $tag is in use; give each transcript a new tag")
            }
            val next = Expectation(tag, text, noRun, now + seconds * 1_000L)
            if (next.words == 0) throw HookError("add -Text with words")
            expectation = next
            listening.transcripts = { event -> check(graph, event) }
        }
        return "expecting tag=$tag seconds=$seconds"
    }

    private fun result(tag: String): String {
        val now = SystemClock.elapsedRealtime()
        synchronized(lock) {
            val current = expectation?.takeIf { it.tag == tag } ?: throw HookError("no expectation has the tag $tag")
            if (current.waiting(now)) return "waiting tag=$tag"
            val match = current.match
            val run = !current.noRun
            val number = if (run) VoiceHooks.tags.tagged(tag)?.command ?: 0 else 0
            val (kind, outcome) = if (run) VoiceHooks.kindAndOutcome(graph, number) else (match?.kind ?: "none") to "none"
            val right = when {
                run -> "-"
                match != null -> match.right
                isCommand(current.expected) -> "0"
                else -> "-"
            }
            return "tag=$tag matched=${match != null} words=${current.words} errors=${current.errors} stray=${current.stray} " +
                "split=${current.split} speechEnded=${match?.speechEnded ?: 0} closed=${match?.closed ?: 0} " +
                "transcribed=${match?.transcribed ?: 0} run=$run command=$number kind=$kind outcome=$outcome right=$right"
        }
    }

    companion object {
        val COMMANDS = setOf("VOICE_EXPECT")

        private val lock = Any()

        // The newest expectation; its answer stays until the next one.
        private var expectation: Expectation? = null

        private val TAG = Regex("[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,32}")
        private const val DEFAULT_SECONDS = 15
        private const val MAX_SECONDS = 60
        private const val EXTRA_TEXT = "text"
        private const val EXTRA_TAG = "tag"
        private const val EXTRA_SECONDS = "seconds"
        private const val EXTRA_RESULT = "result"
        private const val EXTRA_NO_RUN = "noRun"

        // Decides for each transcript of a voice session whether it goes on to the Orchestrator, on the session's thread.
        private fun check(graph: AppGraph, event: Event.Heard): Boolean {
            val now = SystemClock.elapsedRealtime()
            synchronized(lock) {
                val current = expectation ?: return true
                if (!current.holds(now)) return true
                val noRun = current.noRun
                val verdict: (String) -> Pair<String, String> = { heard -> if (noRun) resolve(graph, current.expected, heard) else "none" to "-" }
                if (!current.offer(event.text, event.endedAt, event.closedAt, now, verdict)) return false
                if (noRun) return false
                try {
                    VoiceHooks.tag(graph, current.tag, event)
                } catch (e: HookError) {
                    // A tagged text with the same tag came meanwhile; the match runs untagged, and its answer says command=0.
                }
                // Process rule 10: a heard "yes, call" never reaches Kestrel through a hook on a phone, also as a match. Its tag
                // stays, with no command, so that voice-state -Tag answers role=none.
                if (VoiceHooks.refused(event.text, Build.HARDWARE)) {
                    VoiceHooks.tags.dropped(event)
                    return false
                }
                return true
            }
        }

        // The kind of command that [heard] parses to, and 1 when it leads to the same action as [expected] with the screen's
        // labels, 0 when not, - when [expected] is no instant command, as voice-eval's command= (design spec, section 16.4).
        private fun resolve(graph: AppGraph, expected: String, heard: String): Pair<String, String> {
            val said = CommandParser.parse(heard)
            val kind = said?.javaClass?.simpleName ?: "none"
            val wanted = CommandParser.parse(expected)
            if (wanted == null || wanted is Command.Bare) return kind to "-"
            val labels = graph.screen.value?.snapshots?.latest()?.snapshot?.let { BoostWordBudget.screenNames(it) }.orEmpty()
            return kind to if (said != null && Commands.sameAction(wanted, said, labels)) "1" else "0"
        }

        private fun isCommand(text: String): Boolean = CommandParser.parse(text).let { it != null && it !is Command.Bare }
    }
}
```

```kotlin
    @Test
    fun anExpectedClipIsMatchedOnThePhoneAndRunsWithItsTagWhileAStrayIsDropped() {
        VoiceTests.assumeModels(Engine.VAD, Engine.KWS, Engine.ASR, Engine.TTS)
        VoiceTests.grantListeningPermissions()
        val previous = listening.microphones
        val tag = "air-${System.nanoTime()}"
        assertEquals(
            HookReply(HookReply.FAILED, "error: Kestrel's voice session is not open"),
            hook("VOICE_EXPECT", "text" to "Scroll down.", "tag" to tag),
        )
        try {
            ActivityScenario.launch(MainActivity::class.java).use {
                // Kestrel listens through the silent clip microphone, into which voice-clip says texts in Kestrel's voice.
                assertTrue(hook("VOICE_CLIP", quiet = true).text.startsWith("quiet: "))
                assertEquals("sent toggle", hook("VOICE", "event" to "toggle").text)
                assertTrue("The voice session did not open", VoiceTests.waitUntil(60_000) { listening.sessionOpen.value })

                assertEquals("expecting tag=$tag seconds=15", hook("VOICE_EXPECT", "text" to "Scroll down.", "tag" to tag).text)
                assertEquals(Activity.RESULT_OK, hook("VOICE_CLIP", "text" to "Hello there, how are you today?").code)
                assertEquals(Activity.RESULT_OK, hook("VOICE_CLIP", "text" to "Scroll down.").code)

                val done = VoiceTests.waitUntil(30_000) { !hook("VOICE_EXPECT", "tag" to tag, result = true).text.startsWith("waiting") }
                assertTrue("The expectation did not end", done)
                // The match may still wait in the Orchestrator's queue; its command shows once it has been handled.
                val handled = VoiceTests.waitUntil(10_000) { !hook("VOICE_STATE", "tag" to tag).text.contains(" role=pending ") }
                assertTrue("The Orchestrator did not handle the match", handled)
                val answer = hook("VOICE_EXPECT", "tag" to tag, result = true).text
                val line = Regex(
                    "tag=$tag matched=true words=2 errors=[01] stray=[12] split=0 speechEnded=(\\d+) closed=(\\d+) transcribed=(\\d+) " +
                        "run=true command=\\d+ kind=Scroll outcome=\\w+ right=-",
                )
                val found = line.matchEntire(answer)
                assertTrue(answer, found != null)
                val (ended, closed, transcribed) = found!!.destructured
                // The phone's own times: the speech ends, then the pause, then the text comes.
                assertTrue(answer, ended.toLong() in 1..closed.toLong() && closed.toLong() <= transcribed.toLong())
                assertTrue(hook("VOICE_STATE", "tag" to tag).text.startsWith("tag=$tag role=command "))
                assertTrue("Kestrel still speaks", VoiceTests.waitUntil(20_000) { !listening.state.value.speaking })
                assertEquals("sent off", hook("VOICE", "event" to "off").text)
                assertTrue("The voice session did not close", VoiceTests.waitUntil(30_000) { !listening.sessionOpen.value })
            }
        } finally {
            hook("VOICE_CLIP", clear = true)
            listening.microphones = previous
        }
    }

    @Test
    fun theStateAnswersForACommandThatHasNotStartedWithoutAKindOrAnOutcome() {
```

### Task 4: Checks of the screen on the phone (H3) and the screen layer's state (H4)

**Files:**
- Create: `app/src/debug/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/debug/ScreenQuery.kt`, `app/src/debug/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/debug/ScreenHooks.kt`
- Modify: `app/src/debug/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/debug/TestHooks.kt` (dispatch and `state`)
- Modify: `scripts/hook.ps1`, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-12-kestrel-design.md` (16.1)
- Test: `app/src/testDebug/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/debug/ScreenQueryTest.kt` (new), `app/src/androidTest/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/debug/TestHookTest.kt`, `scripts/tests/Hook.Tests.ps1`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 2's `CommandTags.hash(text)`, `VoiceHooks.tag(graph, tag, event)`, `VoiceHooks.refused(text, hardware)`, `VoiceHooks.YES_CALL_REFUSED`; `ScreenSnapshot.reachable(element)`, `DeviceCommands.frontApp(windows)`, `TextFields.focused(snapshot)`, `ScreenSession.text.read(key)` (`FieldState`), `ScreenSession.numberBadges`, `grid`, `actions.zoomScale`, `system.magnifiedCentre()`; `Normalizer.normalize(text)` and `Normalizer.words(text)`.
- Produces: `internal object ScreenQuery` with `data class Filter(packageName, viewId, label, contains, className, states)`, `val STATES`, `fun states(text: String?): Set<String>`, `fun matches(snapshot, filter): List<ScreenElement>`, `fun firstName(element): String?` and `fun answer(snapshot, badges, filter): String`, with the anchor `// sub-project 5B` in its filter's check, where sub-project 5B would add a kind of match (H3 already matches by "text contains" with `-Contains`); `internal class ScreenHooks(graph)` with the commands `SCREEN_HAS` and `FIELD`; `state`'s fields `gridSize`, `magnification` and `keyboard`; `hook.ps1`'s `screen-has`, `field`, `-Package`, `-ViewId`, `-Label`, `-Contains`, `-ClassName`, `-State` and `-Template`. The answers are those of `plan-4d-interfaces.md`, section 5 (the class filter's parameter is `-ClassName`, because `class` is a keyword of PowerShell).

Hooks H3 and H4 of the map. A check such as "did the page Display open", "is the notification expanded" or "which overlay number has the slider" must not bring the owner's screen text to the PC, and the existing `snapshot` hook brings the whole screen. `screen-has` filters Kestrel's own snapshot on the phone and answers only the count of the matches, the first match's overlay number and bounds, the hash of the first name inside it (so that a list's first row can be compared before and after a scroll), and the package and title hash of the page in front. With `-Template 'long press {label}' -Tag k` it builds a transcript from the first match's name on the phone and sends it tagged, so that a label of the owner's home screen never reaches the PC. `field` compares the focused text field with an expected text on the phone. `state` also names the grid's size, the magnification and whether the keyboard shows (H4, moved here from Task 2 because it reads the screen layer).

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.app.debug

import dev.kestrel.screen.model.IntRect
import dev.kestrel.screen.model.ScreenElement
import dev.kestrel.screen.model.ScreenSnapshot
import dev.kestrel.screen.model.WindowKind
import dev.kestrel.screen.model.WindowSummary
import dev.kestrel.screen.overlay.Badge
import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertNull
import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
import org.junit.Test

/** Hook H3 of plan 4D: which elements match, and an answer of counts, numbers, bounds and hashes only. */
class ScreenQueryTest {
    private val settings = "com.android.settings"
    private val app = WindowSummary(1, WindowKind.APPLICATION, packageName = settings, title = "Display", bounds = IntRect(0, 0, 1080, 2400), active = true)
    private val keyboard = WindowSummary(
        2, WindowKind.INPUT_METHOD, packageName = "com.samsung.android.honeyboard", bounds = IntRect(0, 1600, 1080, 2400), layer = 2,
    )
    private val elements = listOf(
        ScreenElement(
            key = "list", windowId = 1, packageName = settings, className = "androidx.recyclerview.widget.RecyclerView",
            viewId = "$settings:id/recycler_view", bounds = IntRect(0, 200, 1080, 1600), scrollable = true,
        ),
        ScreenElement(
            key = "row", windowId = 1, parent = 0, packageName = settings, className = "android.widget.LinearLayout",
            label = "Brightness level", bounds = IntRect(0, 200, 1080, 400), clickable = true,
        ),
        ScreenElement(
            key = "slider", windowId = 1, parent = 0, packageName = settings, className = "android.widget.SeekBar",
            bounds = IntRect(0, 400, 1080, 500), slider = true,
        ),
        ScreenElement(
            key = "dark", windowId = 1, parent = 0, packageName = settings, className = "android.widget.Switch", label = "Dark mode",
            bounds = IntRect(0, 500, 1080, 600), checkable = true, checked = true, clickable = true,
        ),
        ScreenElement(
            key = "key-q", windowId = 2, packageName = "com.samsung.android.honeyboard", label = "q", bounds = IntRect(0, 1700, 100, 1800),
            clickable = true,
        ),
    )
    private val snapshot = ScreenSnapshot(0, listOf(keyboard, app), elements)

    private fun keys(filter: ScreenQuery.Filter) = ScreenQuery.matches(snapshot, filter).map { it.key }

    @Test
    fun aFilterMatchesByPackageViewIdNameClassAndStateAndNeverInTheKeyboard() {
        assertEquals(listOf("list", "row", "slider", "dark"), keys(ScreenQuery.Filter(packageName = settings)))
        assertEquals(listOf("list"), keys(ScreenQuery.Filter(viewId = "recycler")))
        assertEquals(listOf("dark"), keys(ScreenQuery.Filter(label = "dark MODE.")))
        assertEquals(listOf("row"), keys(ScreenQuery.Filter(contains = "brightness")))
        assertEquals(emptyList<String>(), keys(ScreenQuery.Filter(contains = "level brightness")))
        assertEquals(listOf("slider"), keys(ScreenQuery.Filter(className = "SeekBar")))
        assertEquals(listOf("dark"), keys(ScreenQuery.Filter(states = setOf("checked", "clickable"))))
        assertEquals(listOf("slider"), keys(ScreenQuery.Filter(states = setOf("slider"))))
        assertEquals(emptyList<String>(), keys(ScreenQuery.Filter(label = "q")))
    }

    @Test
    fun theAnswerHoldsTheFirstMatchsNumberBoundsAndInnerNameAsAHashAndThePageInFront() {
        val badges = listOf(Badge(3, "slider", elements[2].bounds), Badge(4, "dark", elements[3].bounds))
        val page = "app=$settings appTitle=${CommandTags.hash("Display")} windows=2"

        assertEquals(
            "count=1 number=none bounds=0,200,1080,1600 first=${CommandTags.hash("Brightness level")} $page",
            ScreenQuery.answer(snapshot, badges, ScreenQuery.Filter(states = setOf("scrollable"))),
        )
        assertEquals(
            "count=1 number=3 bounds=0,400,1080,500 first=none $page",
            ScreenQuery.answer(snapshot, badges, ScreenQuery.Filter(className = "SeekBar")),
        )
        assertEquals(
            "count=0 number=none bounds=none first=none $page",
            ScreenQuery.answer(snapshot, badges, ScreenQuery.Filter(label = "Bluetooth")),
        )
    }

    @Test
    fun aTemplatesLabelIsTheFirstNameOfAnElementAndTheStatesAreAFixedList() {
        assertEquals("Dark mode", ScreenQuery.firstName(elements[3]))
        assertNull(ScreenQuery.firstName(elements[2]))
        assertEquals(setOf("checked", "slider"), ScreenQuery.states("checked, slider"))
        assertEquals(emptySet<String>(), ScreenQuery.states(null))
        assertTrue(runCatching { ScreenQuery.states("shiny") }.exceptionOrNull() is IllegalArgumentException)
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Create `ScreenQuery`**

Create `app/src/debug/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/debug/ScreenQuery.kt`:

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.app.debug

import dev.kestrel.app.commands.DeviceCommands
import dev.kestrel.commands.text.Normalizer
import dev.kestrel.screen.model.ElementAction
import dev.kestrel.screen.model.ScreenElement
import dev.kestrel.screen.model.ScreenSnapshot
import dev.kestrel.screen.overlay.Badge
import java.util.Collections

/**
 * Hook H3 of plan 4D, screen-has: which elements of Kestrel's snapshot match a filter, answered on the phone as counts,
 * overlay numbers, bounds and hashes, never text (2026-11-20-a71-checks-4d-interfaces.md, section 5). Only elements that a finger can
 * reach count, as for the commands. Pure, so that JVM tests check it on made-up snapshots.
 */
internal object ScreenQuery {
    /** What an element must have; null and empty mean anything. */
    data class Filter(
        val packageName: String? = null,
        val viewId: String? = null,
        val label: String? = null,
        val contains: String? = null,
        val className: String? = null,
        val states: Set<String> = emptySet(),
    ) {
        // The label and the words to contain may be a screen's words, so only their lengths are printed.
        override fun toString() =
            "Filter(package=$packageName, viewId=$viewId, label=${label?.length}, contains=${contains?.length}, " +
                "class=$className, states=$states)"
    }

    val STATES = listOf(
        "checked", "unchecked", "selected", "focused", "editable", "password", "scrollable", "clickable", "actionable",
        "expandable", "collapsible", "dismissable", "slider",
    )

    /** The states of a comma-separated [text]; throws [IllegalArgumentException] for one that is not in [STATES]. */
    fun states(text: String?): Set<String> {
        val states = text?.split(',')?.map { it.trim() }?.filter { it.isNotEmpty() }.orEmpty().toSet()
        require(states.all { it in STATES }) { "add --es state with some of ${STATES.joinToString()}" }
        return states
    }

    /** The reachable elements of [snapshot] that pass [filter], in the snapshot's order. */
    fun matches(snapshot: ScreenSnapshot, filter: Filter): List<ScreenElement> =
        snapshot.elements.filter { snapshot.reachable(it) && passes(it, filter) }

    /** The first name of [element]: its label, text, description or hint, for a template's {label}; never sent off the phone. */
    fun firstName(element: ScreenElement): String? =
        listOfNotNull(element.label, element.text, element.contentDescription, element.hint).firstOrNull { it.isNotBlank() }

    /** The answer of screen-has for [snapshot] with the numbers [badges] on it. */
    fun answer(snapshot: ScreenSnapshot, badges: List<Badge>, filter: Filter): String {
        val found = matches(snapshot, filter)
        val first = found.firstOrNull()
        val number = first?.let { element -> badges.firstOrNull { it.key == element.key }?.number }
        val bounds = first?.bounds?.let { "${it.left},${it.top},${it.right},${it.bottom}" }
        val inner = first?.let { innerName(snapshot, it) }
        val front = DeviceCommands.frontApp(snapshot.windows)
        return "count=${found.size} number=${number ?: "none"} bounds=${bounds ?: "none"} " +
            "first=${inner?.let(CommandTags::hash) ?: "none"} app=${front?.packageName ?: "none"} " +
            "appTitle=${front?.title?.let(CommandTags::hash) ?: "none"} windows=${snapshot.windows.size}"
    }

    private fun passes(element: ScreenElement, filter: Filter): Boolean {
        val packageName = filter.packageName
        if (packageName != null && element.packageName != packageName) return false
        val viewId = filter.viewId
        if (viewId != null && element.viewId?.substringAfter(":id/")?.contains(viewId) != true) return false
        val names = listOfNotNull(element.label, element.text, element.contentDescription, element.hint)
        val label = filter.label
        if (label != null && names.none { Normalizer.normalize(it) == Normalizer.normalize(label) }) return false
        val contains = filter.contains
        if (contains != null) {
            val wanted = Normalizer.words(contains)
            if (wanted.isEmpty() || names.none { Collections.indexOfSubList(Normalizer.words(it), wanted) >= 0 }) return false
        }
        val className = filter.className
        if (className != null) {
            val own = element.className ?: return false
            if (own != className && !own.endsWith(".$className")) return false
        }
        // sub-project 5B
        return filter.states.all { has(element, it) }
    }

    private fun has(element: ScreenElement, state: String): Boolean = when (state) {
        "checked" -> element.checkable && element.checked
        "unchecked" -> element.checkable && !element.checked
        "selected" -> element.selected
        "focused" -> element.focused
        "editable" -> element.editable
        "password" -> element.password
        "scrollable" -> element.scrollable
        "clickable" -> element.clickable
        "actionable" -> element.isActionable
        "expandable" -> ElementAction.EXPAND in element.actions
        "collapsible" -> ElementAction.COLLAPSE in element.actions
        "dismissable" -> ElementAction.DISMISS in element.actions
        "slider" -> element.slider
        else -> false
    }

    // The first name of the first actionable element inside [element] that has one, as a list's first visible row.
    private fun innerName(snapshot: ScreenSnapshot, element: ScreenElement): String? {
        val index = snapshot.elements.indexOf(element)
        if (index < 0) return null
        for (inner in index + 1 until snapshot.elements.size) {
            if (!inside(snapshot, inner, index)) return null
            val candidate = snapshot.elements[inner]
            if (candidate.isActionable) firstName(candidate)?.let { return it }
        }
        return null
    }

    // Whether the element at [index] lies inside the element at [ancestor], at most 20 levels down. The snapshot lists the
    // elements inside an element right after it.
    private fun inside(snapshot: ScreenSnapshot, index: Int, ancestor: Int): Boolean {
        var parent = snapshot.elements[index].parent
        var depth = 0
        while (parent != null && depth < MAX_DEPTH) {
            if (parent == ancestor) return true
            parent = snapshot.elements.getOrNull(parent)?.parent
            depth++
        }
        return false
    }

    private const val MAX_DEPTH = 20
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Create `ScreenHooks` and add the state's fields**

Create `app/src/debug/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/debug/ScreenHooks.kt`:

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.app.debug

import android.content.Intent
import android.os.Build
import dev.kestrel.app.AppGraph
import dev.kestrel.app.commands.TextFields
import dev.kestrel.app.debug.TestHooks.HookError
import dev.kestrel.app.orchestrator.Event
import dev.kestrel.screen.ScreenSession
import kotlinx.coroutines.Dispatchers
import kotlinx.coroutines.withContext

/**
 * Hook H3 of plan 4D: screen-has checks Kestrel's snapshot of the screen on the phone ([ScreenQuery]) and can send a
 * tagged transcript built from a match's name on the phone, and field compares a text field with an expected text on the
 * phone. Their answers hold counts, numbers, bounds, flags and hashes, never screen text (2026-11-20-a71-checks-4d-interfaces.md,
 * section 5).
 */
internal class ScreenHooks(private val graph: AppGraph) {
    suspend fun run(command: String, intent: Intent): String {
        val session = graph.screen.value ?: throw HookError("Kestrel's accessibility service is off")
        return when (command) {
            "SCREEN_HAS" -> screenHas(session, intent)
            "FIELD" -> field(session, intent)
            else -> throw HookError("unknown command '$command'")
        }
    }

    private suspend fun screenHas(session: ScreenSession, intent: Intent): String {
        val states = try {
            ScreenQuery.states(intent.getStringExtra(EXTRA_STATE))
        } catch (e: IllegalArgumentException) {
            throw HookError("add --es state with some of ${ScreenQuery.STATES.joinToString()}")
        }
        val filter = ScreenQuery.Filter(
            packageName = intent.getStringExtra(EXTRA_PACKAGE),
            viewId = intent.getStringExtra(EXTRA_VIEW_ID),
            label = intent.getStringExtra(EXTRA_LABEL),
            contains = intent.getStringExtra(EXTRA_CONTAINS),
            className = intent.getStringExtra(EXTRA_CLASS),
            states = states,
        )
        val template = intent.getStringExtra(EXTRA_TEMPLATE)
        if (template != null && LABEL !in template) throw HookError("add $LABEL to the template")
        val snapshot = session.snapshots.current().snapshot
        val badges = withContext(Dispatchers.Main) { session.numberBadges }
        val answer = ScreenQuery.answer(snapshot, badges, filter)
        if (template == null) return answer
        val tag = intent.getStringExtra(EXTRA_TAG) ?: throw HookError("add --es tag <tag> to send the template")
        // The name stays on the phone: it goes only into the transcript that Kestrel hears.
        val name = ScreenQuery.matches(snapshot, filter).firstOrNull()?.let { ScreenQuery.firstName(it) }
            ?: throw HookError("nothing on the screen matches")
        val text = template.replace(LABEL, name)
        if (VoiceHooks.refused(text, Build.HARDWARE)) throw HookError(VoiceHooks.YES_CALL_REFUSED)
        val event = Event.Heard(text, named = false)
        VoiceHooks.tag(graph, tag, event)
        graph.listening.send(event)
        return "$answer sent=$tag"
    }

    // The focused text field, or the first reachable one whose view ID contains --es viewId, compared with --es expected.
    private suspend fun field(session: ScreenSession, intent: Intent): String {
        val expected = intent.getStringExtra(EXTRA_EXPECTED) ?: throw HookError("add --es expected <text>")
        val viewId = intent.getStringExtra(EXTRA_VIEW_ID)
        val snapshot = session.snapshots.current().snapshot
        val element = if (viewId == null) {
            TextFields.focused(snapshot)
        } else {
            snapshot.elements.firstOrNull {
                it.editable && snapshot.reachable(it) && it.viewId?.substringAfter(":id/")?.contains(viewId) == true
            }
        }
        val state = element?.let { session.text.read(it.key) } ?: return "field=none"
        // A password field shows only dots, so it compares nothing.
        val equal = !state.password && state.text == expected
        return "field=found equal=$equal length=${state.text.length} start=${state.selectionStart} end=${state.selectionEnd} " +
            "password=${state.password}"
    }

    companion object {
        val COMMANDS = setOf("SCREEN_HAS", "FIELD")

        private const val LABEL = "{label}"
        private const val EXTRA_PACKAGE = "package"
        private const val EXTRA_VIEW_ID = "viewId"
        private const val EXTRA_LABEL = "label"
        private const val EXTRA_CONTAINS = "contains"
        private const val EXTRA_CLASS = "class"
        private const val EXTRA_STATE = "state"
        private const val EXTRA_TEMPLATE = "template"
        private const val EXTRA_TAG = "tag"
        private const val EXTRA_EXPECTED = "expected"
    }
}
```

```kotlin
        val session = graph.screen.value ?: return "screen=off testMode=$testMode"
        // Plan 4D, H4: whether an input method's window is on the screen, from Kestrel's current snapshot.
        val keyboard = session.snapshots.current().snapshot.windows.any { it.kind == WindowKind.INPUT_METHOD }
        return withContext(Dispatchers.Main) {
            val grid = session.grid
            "screen=on testMode=$testMode numbers=${session.numberBadges.size} labels=${onOff(session.labelsShowing)} " +

                "gridSize=${grid?.let { "${it.rows}x${it.columns}" } ?: "none"} " +
                "magnification=${magnification(session.actions.zoomScale, session.system.magnifiedCentre())} " +
                "keyboard=${if (keyboard) "shown" else "hidden"}"
        }
    }

    // The scale with two decimals and the centre in screen pixels while the screen is magnified, as "2.00@540,1200".
    private fun magnification(scale: Float, centre: Pair<Float, Float>?): String =
        if (scale > 1f && centre != null) {
            String.format(Locale.ROOT, "%.2f@%d,%d", scale, centre.first.roundToInt(), centre.second.roundToInt())
        } else {
            "none"
        }
```

```kotlin
    fun theStateNamesTheGridAndScreenHasCountsWithoutText() = runBlocking {
```

```powershell
# Checks of the screen on the phone (plan 4D; 2026-11-20-a71-checks-4d-interfaces.md, section 5), which answer no screen text:
#   screen-has [-Package p] [-ViewId s] [-Label t] [-Contains t] [-ClassName c] [-State list] [-Template 'text {label}' -Tag k]

#                                           given, and answers the first match's overlay number and bounds, the hash of
#                                           the first name inside it, and the package and title hash of the page in
#                                           front; -State takes checked, unchecked, selected, focused, editable,
#                                           password, scrollable, clickable, actionable, expandable, collapsible,
#                                           dismissable or slider, separated by commas; -Template sends the text, with
#                                           {label} replaced on the phone by the first match's name, as a transcript
#                                           with the tag k
#   field -Expected 'text' [-ViewId s]      compares the focused text field, or the first one whose view ID contains s,
#                                           with the text, and answers whether it is equal, its length, its selection
#                                           and whether it is a password field
# -Number picks an element by the number that the numbers overlay shows on it.
```

### Task 5: The One UI fixes and the clipboard

**Files:**
- Modify: `app/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/commands/DeviceCommands.kt` (Bluetooth's tile, the volume calls)
- Modify: `app/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/commands/TextFields.kt` (`focused`), `app/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/commands/TextCommands.kt` (`focusedState`)
- Create: `core/screen/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/screen/act/InputTypes.kt`
- Modify: `core/screen/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/screen/act/TextEditor.kt` (`focusField`, `multiLine`, `newLine`)
- Modify: `app/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/commands/ScreenCommands.kt` (`swipeFromEdge`), `app/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/commands/CommandTexts.kt`
- Modify: `app/src/debug/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/debug/SessionHooks.kt` (`CLIPBOARD`)
- Modify: `scripts/hook.ps1`, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-12-kestrel-design.md` (9.7, 16.1, Appendix A)
- Test: `app/src/test/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/commands/PhoneDevicesTest.kt`, `app/src/test/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/commands/TextFieldsTest.kt`, `app/src/test/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/commands/EdgeSwipeTest.kt` (new), `core/screen/src/test/kotlin/dev/kestrel/screen/act/InputTypesTest.kt` (new), `app/src/androidTest/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/debug/ClipboardHookTest.kt` (new; in `dev.kestrel.app.debug`, which no script runs on the A71, and only on an emulator), `scripts/tests/Hook.Tests.ps1`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `ScreenTargets.names` (unchanged since 366d867); Task 2's `DeviceCommands.ROUTE_TILE` and `bluetoothResult(changed, route)`, `SessionHooks.EXTRA_ON` and the `CHECK_NOTICE` branch; `DeviceCommands.namesRadio`, `awaitTile`, `awaitState`; `TextEditor.read(key)` and its private `field(key)`, `focus(field)` and `startedEditor(field)`; `Timeline.settingsInFront`.
- Produces: `DeviceCommands.bluetoothTile(snapshot: ScreenSnapshot, element: ScreenElement): Boolean` and `DeviceCommands.adjustments(direction: Int): List<Pair<Int, Int>>`; `TextFields.focused` also gives the only enabled text field inside the focused element that is no text field, while the keyboard shows; `suspend fun TextEditor.focusField(key: String): Boolean`; `internal object InputTypes { fun multiLine(inputType: Int, imeOptions: Int): Boolean; fun breakByAction(inputType: Int, imeOptions: Int): Boolean }`; `internal fun edgeSwipeHint(edge: Edge, buttons: Boolean?, changed: Boolean): String?` and `internal fun navigationButtons(mode: Int?): Boolean?` in `ScreenCommands.kt`; `CommandTexts.EDGE_BUTTONS_BACK` and `EDGE_BUTTONS_HOME`; the hook command `CLIPBOARD` and `hook.ps1`'s `clipboard`, `-Save` and `-Restore` (`plan-4d-interfaces.md`, section 6).

inv-oneui-a71 found four One UI failures and one rule for the clipboard; this task fixes each, with a JVM test that fails before the fix, and lists the automatic A71 checks that the report names, which Task 8 of this plan runs (below; nothing to do for them in this task).

1. **Bluetooth.** One UI 5.1's Quick Settings tiles are clickable layouts with a state description, not checkable switches, so Kestrel found no tile and opened Bluetooth's page. A click on the tile's outer layout is accepted and does nothing; the inner button (a `ViewGroup` "Bluetooth,Off,Button" that offers FOCUS and CLICK) toggles it, and the label group opens the tile's details. Kestrel now also takes that inner button, clicks it once because Android's state already differs, and goes on to the settings page when the state did not change within 6 s.
2. **The ring and notification volumes, and "unmute the ringer".** One UI's AudioService skips a ring-stream or notification-stream change that carries `FLAG_SHOW_UI` while the volume panel is hidden, and only shows the panel. Kestrel now changes the stream without flags and then shows the panel with `ADJUST_SAME` and `FLAG_SHOW_UI`. "Mute the alarm" is Kestrel's intended refusal and stays.
3. **Collapse** needs no fix (8 of 8 worked); Task 8's check reads the result of its own tagged command (Task 2).
4. **Samsung Notes.** Samsung Notes keeps the input focus on the ScrollView around its editor, so Kestrel saw no focused field, dictation was off, and the text commands fell back to ignored bare labels. `TextFields.focused` now also takes the only enabled text field inside the focused element while the keyboard shows, and the text commands focus it before they read it. The note's body says it has one line, but its input's editor action is "none", whose Enter key submits nothing, so `InputTypes.multiLine` takes it as a field with more lines. Kestrel types the line break there as text (`commitText("\n")`), never with the Enter key, because a one-line field with the action "none" may take Enter as "send" (`InputTypes.breakByAction`); the read-back of the line break stays.
5. **The edge swipe.** With the navigation bar's buttons, Android 13 has no back gesture from the edge, so the swipe reached only the app. The swipe stays, because apps may use it; when it changed nothing and the bar has buttons, Kestrel says and shows "The navigation bar has buttons, so this swipe doesn't go back. Say 'go back'." ("go home" for the bottom edge).
6. **The clipboard.** The investigation's "copy" replaced the A71's clipboard with test text, and Android 13 does not let the shell read the old clip. The new hook `clipboard -Save` keeps a copy of the clipboard in Kestrel's memory while Kestrel's settings are in front, where Android lets Kestrel read it, and answers only its kind; `clipboard -Restore` puts it back afterwards, which Android allows from the background. Android answers a refused read and an empty clipboard alike (null), so the hook then answers `clip=unknown`, saves nothing, and a later restore changes nothing: it never empties the clipboard. A second `-Save` keeps a copy that waits (`clip=held`); a put-back that Android refuses, as for a picture whose access ended, answers `restored=failed` and forgets the copy, because Android never gives that access back, so that a later save takes the owner's newer clip. Every check of plan 4D that copies saves it first and puts it back after (Tasks 8 and 9). The hook's device test runs only on an emulator and puts back the clip that the device had, so that no test touches the owner's clipboard.

The A71 checks of these fixes, which Task 8 of this plan runs through the hooks of Tasks 2 to 5, each with every setting put back:

- **Bluetooth (C62, C63):** note `settings get global bluetooth_on`; `logcat -b events -c`; send "turn off Bluetooth" or "turn on Bluetooth" (the one that changes it) with a tag; pass when `voice-state -Tag` gives `outcome=done detail=tile`, `bluetooth_on` changed, `logcat -b events -d` shows `notification_panel_revealed`, and `logcat -d` shows no `BluetoothSettingsActivity`; send the opposite command and check `bluetooth_on` against the noted value.
- **Ring and notification volumes, "unmute the ringer" (C64, C67, C68, C77):** note `settings get global mode_ringer` and `cmd media_session volume --stream N --get` for streams 2 and 5; wait until `dumpsys audio` shows the volume controller with `mVisible=false`; `logcat -c`; send "turn the ring volume up"; pass when the outcome is `done`, stream 2 rose by one and `logcat -d` has no `bypass adjustStreamVolume` line; the same for "down" and for the notification volume; then "silence" (`mode_ringer` 1), the hidden panel again, "unmute the ringer", and pass when `mode_ringer` is 2. Put back each volume with `cmd media_session volume --stream N --set <noted>`, and the noted ringer mode as Task 8's restore does. "Mute the alarm" (C75) passes with `outcome=failed` and `said` holding the hash of `Android doesn't let me mute the alarm volume.`.
- **Collapse (C35, C36):** `check-notice -On true`, "show notifications", then 5 times "expand Kestrel check" and "collapse Kestrel check", each tagged; pass when each ends `done` and, after each collapse, `screen-has -Package com.android.systemui -Contains 'kestrel check' -State expandable` counts 1 and `-State collapsible` counts 0; end with "dismiss Kestrel check" and `check-notice` answering `checkNotice=gone`.
- **Samsung Notes (C88 to C99, C104):** `clipboard -Save` while Kestrel's settings are in front (`am start -n dev.kestrel/dev.kestrel.app.MainActivity`) before the step; `am start -n com.samsung.android.app.notes/.memolist.MemoListActivity`; `screen-has -ViewId inflate_note_fab` and `input tap` at its bounds' centre; `input tap` in the middle of the body; then the text step's commands, tagged, each checked with `field -Expected '<the expected text>'` (for example `Hello, how are you?` after the first, a line break after "new line", `P123` after "capital p one two three"); end with "select all", "delete", Back twice, and `screen-has -Contains '<a test word>'` counting 0 in the memo list; then `clipboard -Restore`, and when `-Save` answered `unknown` or `other`, or `-Restore` answered `failed`, the result line says that the clipboard may hold the check's test text. Do not use the SEND intent, which asks for a permission.
- **Edge swipe (C26):** read `settings get secure navigation_mode` (no change of the mode without the owner); in gesture mode (2), "swipe from the left edge" on Settings > Display must bring back Settings' first page (`dumpsys activity activities` and `screen-has`'s `appTitle`); in button mode (0 or 1), it must leave Display in front, end `done`, and `said` must hold the hash of `The navigation bar has buttons, so this swipe doesn't go back. Say 'go back'.`.
- **Every check that copies (C92, C117):** `clipboard -Save` first and `clipboard -Restore` after, as for Samsung Notes; the owner's start prompt says that a copy check runs, and a result line says so when the clipboard could not be saved or put back.

```kotlin
    fun theOnlyFieldInsideAFocusedContainerCountsWhileTheKeyboardShows() {
        // Samsung Notes: the input focus is on the ScrollView that holds the note's body, and the body reports no focus.
        val scroll = ScreenElement(
            key = "scroll", windowId = 1, packageName = "dev.app", className = "android.widget.ScrollView",

        )

        val title = field("title", "Title", 100)

        assertEquals(body, TextFields.focused(snapshot(scroll, body, title, keyboardShows = true)))
        // Without the keyboard, or with two fields inside the container, none counts.
        assertNull(TextFields.focused(snapshot(scroll, body, title)))
        assertNull(TextFields.focused(snapshot(scroll, body, field("second", null, 600).copy(parent = 0), keyboardShows = true)))
        // A field that has the focus itself comes first.
        val focusedTitle = title.copy(focused = true)
        assertEquals(focusedTitle, TextFields.focused(snapshot(scroll, body, focusedTitle, keyboardShows = true)))
    }
```

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.app.commands

import dev.kestrel.commands.Edge
import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertNull
import org.junit.Test

/** A swipe from an edge while the navigation bar has buttons (inv-oneui-a71, 5). */
class EdgeSwipeTest {
    @Test
    fun withButtonsASwipeFromASideOrTheBottomThatChangedNothingSaysWhatToSayInstead() {
        assertEquals(CommandTexts.EDGE_BUTTONS_BACK, edgeSwipeHint(Edge.LEFT, buttons = true, changed = false))
        assertEquals(CommandTexts.EDGE_BUTTONS_BACK, edgeSwipeHint(Edge.RIGHT, buttons = true, changed = false))
        assertEquals(CommandTexts.EDGE_BUTTONS_HOME, edgeSwipeHint(Edge.BOTTOM, buttons = true, changed = false))
        // The shade comes from the top in every mode, a swipe that changed the screen did something, and gestures go back.
        assertNull(edgeSwipeHint(Edge.TOP, buttons = true, changed = false))
        assertNull(edgeSwipeHint(Edge.LEFT, buttons = true, changed = true))
        assertNull(edgeSwipeHint(Edge.LEFT, buttons = false, changed = false))
        assertNull(edgeSwipeHint(Edge.LEFT, buttons = null, changed = false))
        // Android's navigation modes: 0 three buttons, 1 two buttons, 2 gestures.
        assertEquals(true, navigationButtons(0))
        assertEquals(true, navigationButtons(1))
        assertEquals(false, navigationButtons(2))
        assertNull(navigationButtons(null))
        assertNull(navigationButtons(7))
    }
}
```

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.screen.act

import android.text.InputType
import android.view.inputmethod.EditorInfo
import org.junit.Assert.assertFalse
import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
import org.junit.Test

/** Which inputs take the Enter key as a line break (design spec, section 9.7; inv-oneui-a71, 4). */
class InputTypesTest {
    @Test
    fun anInputTakesALineBreakWithTheMultiLineFlagOrWithoutAnEditorAction() {
        // Samsung Notes' body on the A71: text with capital sentences, IME_ACTION_NONE and IME_FLAG_NO_EXTRACT_UI.
        assertTrue(InputTypes.multiLine(0x4001, 0x10000001))
        assertTrue(InputTypes.multiLine(InputType.TYPE_CLASS_TEXT or InputType.TYPE_TEXT_FLAG_MULTI_LINE, EditorInfo.IME_ACTION_DONE))
        assertFalse(InputTypes.multiLine(InputType.TYPE_CLASS_TEXT, EditorInfo.IME_ACTION_DONE))
        assertFalse(InputTypes.multiLine(InputType.TYPE_CLASS_TEXT, EditorInfo.IME_ACTION_UNSPECIFIED))
        assertFalse(InputTypes.multiLine(InputType.TYPE_CLASS_PHONE, EditorInfo.IME_ACTION_NONE))
        // Only the action "none" makes it multi-line: the line break is typed as text, never with the Enter key.
        assertTrue(InputTypes.breakByAction(0x4001, 0x10000001))
        assertFalse(InputTypes.breakByAction(InputType.TYPE_CLASS_TEXT or InputType.TYPE_TEXT_FLAG_MULTI_LINE, EditorInfo.IME_ACTION_NONE))
        assertFalse(InputTypes.breakByAction(InputType.TYPE_CLASS_TEXT, EditorInfo.IME_ACTION_DONE))
    }
}
```

```kotlin
        /**
         * Whether [element] is the part of Bluetooth's Quick Settings tile that toggles it: AOSP's checkable tile named
         * Bluetooth, or, on One UI, whose tiles are not checkable, the clickable part inside the tile that offers FOCUS and
         * CLICK and whose own text or description, before its first comma, is Bluetooth ("Bluetooth,Off,Button"). Never One
         * UI's outer layout, whose click Android takes and One UI ignores, nor its label group, which opens the tile's
         * details (inv-oneui-a71, 1). The state's words, such as "Off", are translated, so they never count.
         */
        fun bluetoothTile(snapshot: ScreenSnapshot, element: ScreenElement): Boolean {
            if (element.packageName != SYSTEM_UI || !element.enabled) return false
            if (element.checkable) return namesRadio(snapshot, element, RadioKind.BLUETOOTH)
            return element.clickable && ElementAction.FOCUS in element.actions && ElementAction.CLICK in element.actions &&
                element.viewId?.endsWith(":id/label_group") != true &&
                listOfNotNull(element.text, element.contentDescription).any { isRadioName(it, RadioKind.BLUETOOTH) }
        }
```

```kotlin
    /**
     * The text field that has the focus, outside the keyboard's window, in the window that has the input focus when a
     * window has it. WebView marks its focused field this way too (P4).
     */
    fun focused(snapshot: ScreenSnapshot): ScreenElement? {
        val keyboard = keyboardWindows(snapshot)
        val fields = snapshot.elements.filter { it.editable && it.focused && it.enabled && it.windowId !in keyboard }
        // A field keeps its focus while another window has the input focus, as in split screen.
        val input = snapshot.windows.firstOrNull { it.focused }?.id ?: return fields.firstOrNull()
        return fields.firstOrNull { it.windowId == input }
    }
```

with:

```kotlin
    /**
     * The text field that has the focus, outside the keyboard's window, in the window that has the input focus when a
     * window has it. WebView marks its focused field this way too (P4). Without one, while the keyboard shows, the only
     * enabled text field inside the focused element that is no text field: Samsung Notes keeps the focus on the
     * ScrollView around its editor, whose node reports no focus until Kestrel focuses it (inv-oneui-a71, 4), which the
     * text commands do before they read it (TextCommands.focusedState).
     */
    fun focused(snapshot: ScreenSnapshot): ScreenElement? {
        val keyboard = keyboardWindows(snapshot)
        val fields = snapshot.elements.filter { it.editable && it.focused && it.enabled && it.windowId !in keyboard }
        // A field keeps its focus while another window has the input focus, as in split screen.
        val input = snapshot.windows.firstOrNull { it.focused }?.id
        val field = if (input == null) fields.firstOrNull() else fields.firstOrNull { it.windowId == input }
        return field ?: heldField(snapshot, input, keyboard)
    }

    // The only enabled text field inside the first focused element that is no text field, in the window [input] when one
    // has the input focus, while the keyboard shows.
    private fun heldField(snapshot: ScreenSnapshot, input: Int?, keyboard: Set<Int>): ScreenElement? {
        if (!keyboardShows(snapshot)) return null
        val holder = snapshot.elements.indexOfFirst {
            it.focused && !it.editable && it.windowId !in keyboard && (input == null || it.windowId == input)
        }
        if (holder < 0) return null
        return snapshot.elements.filterIndexed { index, element ->
            element.editable && element.enabled && inside(snapshot, index, holder)
        }.singleOrNull()
    }

    // Whether the element at [index] lies inside the element at [ancestor], at most 20 levels down.
    private fun inside(snapshot: ScreenSnapshot, index: Int, ancestor: Int): Boolean {
        var parent = snapshot.elements[index].parent
        var depth = 0
        while (parent != null && depth < MAX_DEPTH) {
            if (parent == ancestor) return true
            parent = snapshot.elements.getOrNull(parent)?.parent
            depth++
        }
        return false
    }

    private const val MAX_DEPTH = 20
```


with:

```kotlin
    private suspend fun focusedState(screen: ScreenSession, field: ScreenElement): FieldState? {
        val state = screen.text.read(field.key) ?: return null
        if (state.focused) return state
        // A field that TextFields found inside the focused container gets the focus first (inv-oneui-a71, 4). A field that
        // the snapshot showed with the focus and has lost it may be a look-alike now, so it stays untouched.
        if (field.focused || !screen.text.focusField(field.key)) return null
        return screen.text.read(field.key)?.takeIf { it.focused }
    }
```

Create `core/screen/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/screen/act/InputTypes.kt`:

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.screen.act

import android.text.InputType
import android.view.inputmethod.EditorInfo

/** What an app's input says about its field (design spec, section 9.7), from the numbers of its EditorInfo. */
internal object InputTypes {
    /**
     * Whether an input of [inputType] and [imeOptions] takes the Enter key as a line break: a text input with the
     * multi-line flag, or one whose editor action is IME_ACTION_NONE, whose Enter key submits nothing, as Samsung Notes'
     * body (inv-oneui-a71, 4). The IME multi-line flag means that the field itself has one line (InputType).
     */
    fun multiLine(inputType: Int, imeOptions: Int): Boolean =
        inputType and InputType.TYPE_MASK_CLASS == InputType.TYPE_CLASS_TEXT &&
            (inputType and InputType.TYPE_TEXT_FLAG_MULTI_LINE != 0 || imeOptions and EditorInfo.IME_MASK_ACTION == EditorInfo.IME_ACTION_NONE)

    /**
     * Whether only the editor action "none" makes the input take a line break, because it has no multi-line flag. Such a
     * field may still have one line, whose app may take the Enter key as "send", so Kestrel types the line break as text
     * there and never presses Enter (plan 4D).
     */
    fun breakByAction(inputType: Int, imeOptions: Int): Boolean =
        multiLine(inputType, imeOptions) && inputType and InputType.TYPE_TEXT_FLAG_MULTI_LINE == 0
}
```

```kotlin
            editor.inputType and InputType.TYPE_TEXT_FLAG_MULTI_LINE != 0
```

with:

```kotlin
internal fun screenCommands(environment: CommandEnvironment): CommandPart = ScreenCommands(environment)

/**
 * What Kestrel says after a swipe from [edge] that [changed] nothing while the navigation bar has buttons ([buttons]):
 * Android has no back gesture from a side and no home gesture from the bottom then, and the swipe reached only the app
 * (inv-oneui-a71, 5). Null otherwise, also when the mode is not known.
 */
internal fun edgeSwipeHint(edge: Edge, buttons: Boolean?, changed: Boolean): String? = when {
    buttons != true || changed -> null
    edge == Edge.LEFT || edge == Edge.RIGHT -> CommandTexts.EDGE_BUTTONS_BACK
    edge == Edge.BOTTOM -> CommandTexts.EDGE_BUTTONS_HOME
    else -> null
}

/** Whether Android's navigation [mode] has buttons: 0 three buttons, 1 two buttons, 2 gestures; null when not known. */
internal fun navigationButtons(mode: Int?): Boolean? = when (mode) {
    0, 1 -> true
    2 -> false
    else -> null
}

private const val NAVIGATION_MODE = "navigation_mode"
```

```kotlin
    /** "Swipe from the bottom edge" and the other edges: from one pixel inside the edge, a third of the screen inwards. */
    private suspend fun swipeFromEdge(screen: ScreenSession, edge: Edge): CommandResult {
        val line = GesturePaths.fromEdge(screen.screenBounds, screenEdge(edge))
        return done(screen.actions.swipe(line.fromX, line.fromY, line.toX, line.toY))
    }
```

with:

```kotlin
    /**
     * "Swipe from the bottom edge" and the other edges: from one pixel inside the edge, a third of the screen inwards. With
     * the navigation bar's buttons, a swipe that changed nothing says what to say instead (edgeSwipeHint); the swipe stays,
     * because apps may use it, as for a navigation drawer.
     */
    private suspend fun swipeFromEdge(screen: ScreenSession, edge: Edge): CommandResult {
        val line = GesturePaths.fromEdge(screen.screenBounds, screenEdge(edge))
        val before = page(screen.snapshots.current().snapshot)
        val result = screen.actions.swipe(line.fromX, line.fromY, line.toX, line.toY)
        if (result is ActionResult.Failed) return done(result)
        val changed = page(screen.snapshots.current().snapshot) != before
        val hint = edgeSwipeHint(edge, navigationButtons(navigationMode()), changed) ?: return done(result)
        return CommandResult(Outcome.DONE, say = hint, show = hint)
    }

    // What an edge swipe compares: the app window in front with its title, and the actionable elements with their places.
    private fun page(snapshot: ScreenSnapshot): Any = Pair(
        snapshot.windows.firstOrNull { it.kind == WindowKind.APPLICATION && it.active }?.let { it.id to it.title },
        snapshot.elements.filter { it.isActionable }.map { it.key to it.bounds },
    )

    // Android's navigation mode: Settings.Secure's navigation_mode, or else the framework's config_navBarInteractionMode,
    // which the navigation bar's overlays set; null when Kestrel can read neither.
    @SuppressLint("DiscouragedApi")
    private fun navigationMode(): Int? {
        val context = environment.context
        try {
            return Settings.Secure.getInt(context.contentResolver, NAVIGATION_MODE)
        } catch (e: Settings.SettingNotFoundException) {
            // The framework's value below.
        } catch (e: SecurityException) {
            // Android keeps the setting from apps; the framework's value below.
        }
        val id = context.resources.getIdentifier("config_navBarInteractionMode", "integer", "android")
        return if (id != 0) context.resources.getInteger(id) else null
    }
```

```kotlin
        return "checkNotice=${if (shown()) "shown" else "gone"}"
```

```kotlin
    /**
     * The owner's clipboard around a check that copies (plan 4D). --ez save true keeps a copy of the clipboard in this
     * process's memory and answers its kind, text or other. Android 13 lets Kestrel read the clipboard only while its
     * window has the input focus, and answers null both for an empty clipboard and for a refused read, so null, or Kestrel's
     * settings not in front, is "unknown": nothing is saved, and a later restore changes nothing. While a copy from an
     * earlier save waits, a save keeps it and answers clip=held. --ez restore true puts the copy back, which Android allows
     * from the background, and forgets it only once that worked; when Android refuses, as for a picture whose read access
     * ended with its clip, the copy is forgotten too, because Android never gives that access back, and the answer is
     * restored=failed. A restore never empties the clipboard.
     * --ez clear true empties the clipboard, for the emulator's tests. The answers hold kinds only, never the content, and
     * nothing is written anywhere.
     */
    private suspend fun clipboard(intent: Intent): String = withContext(Dispatchers.Main) {
        val manager = context.getSystemService(ClipboardManager::class.java) ?: throw HookError("the clipboard service is missing")
        when {
            intent.getBooleanExtra(EXTRA_SAVE, false) -> {
                if (saved != null) return@withContext "clip=held saved=false"
                if (!graph.timeline.settingsInFront) return@withContext "clip=unknown saved=false"
                val clip = manager.primaryClip?.takeIf { it.itemCount > 0 } ?: return@withContext "clip=unknown saved=false"
                saved = clip
                "clip=${kindOf(clip)} saved=true"
            }
            intent.getBooleanExtra(EXTRA_RESTORE, false) -> {
                val held = saved ?: return@withContext "restored=false"
                try {
                    manager.setPrimaryClip(held)
                } catch (e: SecurityException) {
                    // Android never gives that access back, so the copy is forgotten, and a later save takes the owner's
                    // newer clip.
                    saved = null
                    return@withContext "restored=failed"
                }
                saved = null
                "restored=true"
            }
            intent.getBooleanExtra(EXTRA_CLEAR, false) -> {
                manager.clearPrimaryClip()
                "cleared=true"
            }
            else -> throw HookError("add --ez save true, --ez restore true or --ez clear true")
        }
    }

    private fun kindOf(clip: ClipData): String =
        if (clip.description.hasMimeType(ClipDescription.MIMETYPE_TEXT_PLAIN) || clip.description.hasMimeType(ClipDescription.MIMETYPE_TEXT_HTML)) {
            "text"
        } else {
            "other"
        }
```

Create `app/src/androidTest/kotlin/dev/kestrel/app/debug/ClipboardHookTest.kt`:

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.app.debug

import android.content.ClipData
import android.content.ClipboardManager
import android.content.Intent
import android.os.Build
import android.os.SystemClock
import androidx.test.core.app.ActivityScenario
import androidx.test.platform.app.InstrumentationRegistry
import dev.kestrel.app.KestrelApplication
import dev.kestrel.app.MainActivity
import kotlinx.coroutines.runBlocking
import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
import org.junit.Assume.assumeTrue
import org.junit.Test

/**
 * The clipboard hook of plan 4D. It runs only on an emulator and puts back the clip that the device had, so that no test
 * ever touches the owner's clipboard; it is not in dev.kestrel.app.voice, which scripts\voice-test.ps1 runs on a phone.
 */
class ClipboardHookTest {
    private val context = InstrumentationRegistry.getInstrumentation().targetContext
    private val graph = (context.applicationContext as KestrelApplication).graph

    @Test
    fun theClipboardIsSavedWhileKestrelIsInFrontAndPutBackAfterACheckChangedIt() {
        assumeTrue("Runs only on an emulator", Build.HARDWARE == "ranchu" || Build.HARDWARE == "goldfish")
        val clipboard = context.getSystemService(ClipboardManager::class.java)

        ActivityScenario.launch(MainActivity::class.java).use { scenario ->
            // Android lets Kestrel read the clipboard only while its window has the input focus.
            val focused = waitUntil(5_000) {
                var focus = false
                scenario.onActivity { focus = it.hasWindowFocus() }
                graph.timeline.settingsInFront && focus
            }
            assertTrue("Kestrel's settings do not have the focus", focused)
            var original: ClipData? = null
            scenario.onActivity { original = clipboard.primaryClip }
            try {
                scenario.onActivity { clipboard.setPrimaryClip(ClipData.newPlainText("test", "The test's first clip")) }
                assertEquals("clip=text saved=true", hook("save"))
                // A second save keeps the first copy.
                assertEquals("clip=held saved=false", hook("save"))
                // As a check's "copy" does.
                scenario.onActivity { clipboard.setPrimaryClip(ClipData.newPlainText("test", "The check's text")) }

                assertEquals("restored=true", hook("restore"))
                var text: String? = null
                scenario.onActivity { text = clipboard.primaryClip?.getItemAt(0)?.text?.toString() }
                assertEquals("The test's first clip", text)
                assertEquals("restored=false", hook("restore"))
            } finally {
                scenario.onActivity {
                    val clip = original
                    if (clip != null) clipboard.setPrimaryClip(clip) else clipboard.clearPrimaryClip()
                }
            }
        }
    }

    private fun hook(flag: String): String =
        runBlocking { TestHooks(graph, context).run(Intent(TestHooks.ACTION_PREFIX + "CLIPBOARD").putExtra(flag, true)) }.text

    private fun waitUntil(timeoutMillis: Long, condition: () -> Boolean): Boolean {
        val end = SystemClock.uptimeMillis() + timeoutMillis
        while (SystemClock.uptimeMillis() < end) {
            if (condition()) return true
            Thread.sleep(50)
        }
        return condition()
    }
}
```
