## Counts at the end of Tasks 1 to 5

`:app` JVM tests A₀ + 20 (Task 1: 2; Task 2: 8; Task 3: 3; Task 4: 3; Task 5: 4), `:core:voice` V₀ + 3, `:core:screen` S₀ + 1, the device tests of `:app` O₀ + 6 (Task 2: 3; Task 3: 1; Task 4: 1; Task 5: 1), Pester T₀ + 4 in each shell. `:core:commands` and the other modules are unchanged.

### Task 6: The speaker, the readers of Android's dumps and the check runner

**Files:**
- Create: `scripts/lib/Speaker.ps1`, `scripts/lib/Oracle.ps1`, `scripts/lib/AutoCheck.ps1`
- Modify: `scripts/lib/Session.ps1` (`Read-A71Config`, `Send-TestPin`, `Test-KeyguardOccluded`, `Get-PinPadState` and `Unlock-TestPhone` at its end)
- Test: `scripts/tests/Speaker.Tests.ps1`, `scripts/tests/Oracle.Tests.ps1`, `scripts/tests/AutoCheck.Tests.ps1` (new), `scripts/tests/Session.Tests.ps1`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `scripts\lib\Session.ps1`'s `ConvertFrom-HookPairs` and `ConvertFrom-Uptime`; the hook names and answers of `plan-4d-interfaces.md` sections 1, 2 and 4 (`voice -Send heard -Text -Tag`, `voice-state -Tag`, `voice-expect -Text -Tag -Seconds` and `voice-expect -Result -Tag`), which the runner calls through the caller's `Invoke-Hook([string]$Command, [hashtable]$With)`. The runner needs none of Tasks 2 to 4 to be built: its tests mock `Invoke-Hook`.
- Produces, for Tasks 7 to 10:
  - `Speaker.ps1`: `Get-PcMilliseconds`; `Read-WavFile($Path)` (`SampleRate`, `Channels`, `Samples` as bytes); `ConvertTo-WavBytes($Samples, $SampleRate, $Channels = 1)`; `Write-WavFile($Path, $Samples, $SampleRate, $Channels = 1)`; `Get-ClipSpeechEnd($Path, $ThresholdDb = -40)` (`SpeechEndMs`, `LengthMs`); `Get-ScaledClip($Path, $Gain)`; `Invoke-Clip($Path, $Gain = 1)` (`StartMs`, `EndMs` on `Get-PcMilliseconds`' clock); `Start-ClipLoop($Path, $Gain = 1)` and `Stop-ClipLoop($Player)`; `Get-PhoneClock($ReadUptime, $Reads = 10)` (`OffsetMs`, `ErrorMs`); `Set-SpeakerLevel($Measure, $TargetDb, $Band = 3, $Start = 0.5, $Tries = 6)` (`Gain`, `LevelDb`, `InBand`); the type `KestrelSpeech` (`Scale`, `EndOfLoud`).
  - `Oracle.ps1`: `Get-ResumedPackage`, `Get-HomePackage`, `Get-Wakefulness`, `Test-KeyguardShowing`, `Test-InputShown`, `Get-PlaybackState($Dump, $Package)`, `Test-StreamMuted($Dump, $Stream = 'STREAM_MUSIC')`, `Get-FocusedWindow`, `Get-TextHash($Text)`, `Test-HashedText($Hashes, $Text)`.
  - `AutoCheck.ps1`: `New-CheckTag`, `Test-YesCall`, `ConvertFrom-TaggedState`, `Test-TaggedSettled`, `Wait-TaggedState($Tag, $Until, $TimeoutSeconds = 15)`, `Wait-ExpectResult($Tag, $TimeoutSeconds = 30)`, `ConvertTo-Verdict`, and `Invoke-AutoCheck -Name -Text -Verify [-Before] [-Answer | -Respond] [-RespondAt question|countdown] [-RespondDelay] [-Send] [-Clip { Voice, Text, Path }] [-Play] [-TimeoutSeconds 15] [-Wait { param($Tag, $Seconds) } [-WaitSeconds 600]]`, which returns `Name`, `Path`, `Tag`, `State`, `Heard`, `Waited`, `Passed` and `Line`, and answers "no" to a question that the check did not plan, so that it never takes the next check's words.
  - `Session.ps1`: `Read-A71Config($Path)` (`CallNumber`, `TestContacts`, `SpeakerTargetDb`, and every other key of the file except `pin`), the reader that every part but the unlock uses; `Send-TestPin($Serial, $ConfigPath, [-CheckOnly])`, the only reader of the key `pin`, which with `-CheckOnly` checks it without a device and otherwise types the PIN through its own call of adb and throws only "the unlock failed"; `Test-KeyguardOccluded($Dump)`; `Get-PinPadState($Serial)` (`$true`, `$false` or `$null`); `Unlock-TestPhone($Serial, $ConfigPath)`, which needs `Kestrel.ps1` and `Oracle.ps1`, returns `$true` when the phone is unlocked and `$false` when it typed nothing because the phone did not show its PIN pad, which the scripts report as such, and throws "the unlock failed" after a typing that did not work, and at once in every later call of the run.

The map's speaker library, oracle library and check runner, as libraries that the three session scripts dot-source, so that every helper has its Pester test here and each session script stays a list of checks. Three choices differ from the map. The speaker sets the loudness with a gain on the clip's samples, which `System.Media.SoundPlayer` then plays from memory, instead of Windows Media Player's volume: `SoundPlayer` is in every Windows, the COM object of Windows Media Player needs an optional feature, and a gain is testable; the gain stays at most 1, so the PC's own volume must be high enough, which `Set-SpeakerLevel` says (`InBand=false` at gain 1). The loops over the samples are a small C# type (`Add-Type`), because PowerShell would take minutes for the 30-minute room-sound track. And the runner refuses every "yes, call" in its own code too (process rule 10), not only through the hook, and it cancels at once every countdown to a call that a check did not plan, or that a failed response left running, so that Kestrel does not press Call; such a check fails. Two needs of sub-project 5's design come in too: `ConvertFrom-TaggedState` reads the field `task` of `voice-state -Tag` (`role=task` for a transcript that started a task, `task=0` otherwise, also when an older answer lacks the field), and `Invoke-AutoCheck -Wait` takes a script block, such as one that polls sub-project 5's `agent-task -Result -Tag k`, which the runner calls with the tag and `-WaitSeconds` (at most 600, 10 minutes) instead of waiting for the command's end; there is no `agent-task` hook yet, so its test uses a fake. The libraries run in Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7, so their tests run in both shells, as `scripts\test-scripts.ps1` does; no test plays a sound or needs a device. The owner's settings for the A71 (`corpus\owner\a71-config.json`, which git ignores) are JSON, and later plans add keys to it: `Read-A71Config`, the reader that every part uses, gives every key as it is except `pin`, which it leaves out, and, checked, the number that is fine to call, his consent to the two test contacts, and the phone's target level for the PC's clips, -26 dBFS of the loudest 20 ms frame by default (the map leaves the target to this plan; the air pass's match counts show whether it needs a change). The owner allows the sessions to unlock the A71 with a test PIN (his decision of 2026-11-20), so his two PIN unlocks of the map (C49, S13) leave his step: only `Send-TestPin` reads the key `pin`, each time right before it types it; the scripts, only on the A71, call it at their start with `-CheckOnly`, which throws a clear message, without the PIN, when the file or the key is missing; and `Unlock-TestPhone`, which gets only the file's path, wakes the phone and, when the keyguard shows and no activity shows over it (`Occluded=true`, such as a call's screen), brings up the PIN pad without looking (a swipe up and `wm dismiss-keyguard`), and types the PIN only when `Get-PinPadState` says clearly that the pad shows: `dumpsys activity activities` says that the keyguard shows and is not occluded, and the screen's hierarchy holds the pad's entry (the view IDs `pinEntry` or `keyguard_pin_view` of SystemUI). No dumpsys of Android 13 or 17 tells the PIN pad from the rest of the lock screen, because both are SystemUI's notification shade window, so the hierarchy is read once per unlock: `uiautomator dump` writes it to `/data/local/tmp`, which the shell owns and which exists before the first unlock after a restart (`/sdcard` does not), and only the count of those IDs is read there before the file is deleted, because the hierarchy can hold the lock screen's notifications. Each dump unbinds every accessibility service, Kestrel's too, for 1 to 10 s, which the comment says. A state that Android does not say clearly never types, so the PIN never reaches another app's field, and the unlock also works after a restart, when Android asks for the PIN and not for a swipe. The PIN is typed at most once per unlock, and a typing that did not unlock the phone within 10 s throws "the unlock failed" and makes every later unlock of the run throw at once: One UI locks the phone for a while after 5 wrong PINs, and can reset it after 15 when that is switched on. A PIN must be text in quotes: Windows PowerShell 5.1 reads `{"pin": 0246}` as the number 246, and PowerShell 7's parser repeats the file's text in its error, so the readers replace that error with their own. The PIN never goes through `Invoke-SettingsCommand`, because that helper repeats the whole command in its errors, and the session scripts write those errors into their results: `Send-TestPin` calls adb itself, drops adb's output, clears the PIN and its command after use, and throws only "the unlock failed", whatever went wrong. No script holds the PIN in a variable of its own. Nothing writes the PIN into a result, a log or the console, and a test with a fake adb that fails and repeats its command checks that no output, warning or error holds it.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests of the speaker**

Create `scripts/tests/Speaker.Tests.ps1`:

```powershell
# Tests of scripts\lib\Speaker.ps1, which the A71 sessions use to play the corpus's clips to the phone. No test plays a
# sound. scripts\test-scripts.ps1 runs these tests in Windows PowerShell 5.1 and in PowerShell 7.
[Diagnostics.CodeAnalysis.SuppressMessageAttribute('PSUseDeclaredVarsMoreThanAssignments', '',
    Justification = 'Pester shares the variables of BeforeAll with the It blocks.')]
param()

BeforeAll {
    . "$PSScriptRoot\..\lib\Session.ps1"
    . "$PSScriptRoot\..\lib\Speaker.ps1"

    # 16-bit samples of $Milliseconds ms at 16 kHz, all with the value $Value.
    function New-Samples([int]$Milliseconds, [int16]$Value) {
        $pair = [BitConverter]::GetBytes($Value)
        $bytes = New-Object byte[] ($Milliseconds * 32)
        for ($i = 0; $i -lt $bytes.Length; $i += 2) {
            $bytes[$i] = $pair[0]
            $bytes[$i + 1] = $pair[1]
        }
        , $bytes
    }
}

Describe 'The speaker''s clips' {
    It 'writes and reads a clip, and finds where its speech ends before the silence' {
        $samples = [byte[]]((New-Samples 200 0) + (New-Samples 500 8000) + (New-Samples 300 -20000) + (New-Samples 1000 100))
        $path = Join-Path $TestDrive 'clip.wav'

        Write-WavFile -Path $path -Samples $samples -SampleRate 16000

        $wav = Read-WavFile $path
        $wav.SampleRate | Should -Be 16000
        $wav.Channels | Should -Be 1
        $wav.Samples.Length | Should -Be $samples.Length
        $end = Get-ClipSpeechEnd $path
        $end.SpeechEndMs | Should -Be 1000
        $end.LengthMs | Should -Be 2000
        (Get-ClipSpeechEnd (Join-Path $TestDrive 'clip.wav') -ThresholdDb -60).SpeechEndMs | Should -Be 2000
    }

    It 'refuses a file that is not a 16-bit PCM WAV file' {
        $path = Join-Path $TestDrive 'text.wav'
        [IO.File]::WriteAllText($path, 'not a clip')

        { Read-WavFile $path } | Should -Throw 'Not a WAV file*'
    }

    It 'multiplies the samples by the gain, within the 16-bit range' {
        $path = Join-Path $TestDrive 'loud.wav'
        Write-WavFile -Path $path -Samples ([byte[]]((New-Samples 1 8000) + (New-Samples 1 -30000))) -SampleRate 16000

        $scaled = Read-WavFile -Path $path
        $half = [KestrelSpeech]::Scale($scaled.Samples, 0.5)
        $double = [KestrelSpeech]::Scale($scaled.Samples, 2)

        [BitConverter]::ToInt16($half, 0) | Should -Be 4000
        [BitConverter]::ToInt16($half, 32) | Should -Be -15000
        [BitConverter]::ToInt16($double, 0) | Should -Be 16000
        [BitConverter]::ToInt16($double, 32) | Should -Be -32768
    }
}

Describe 'The phone''s clock and the loudness' {
    It 'finds the offset between this computer''s clock and the phone''s from the read with the shortest round trip' {
        $read = {
            $phone = (Get-PcMilliseconds) + 5000000
            ($phone / 1000).ToString('0.000', [Globalization.CultureInfo]::InvariantCulture) + ' 100.00'
        }

        $clock = Get-PhoneClock -ReadUptime $read -Reads 5

        [math]::Abs($clock.OffsetMs - 5000000) | Should -BeLessThan 50
        $clock.ErrorMs | Should -BeGreaterOrEqual 10
    }

    It 'finds the gain that brings the phone''s level into the band, and says when this computer is too quiet' {
        # The phone hears the clip at -20 dBFS at gain 1.
        $measure = { param($Gain) -20 + 20 * [math]::Log10($Gain) }

        $level = Set-SpeakerLevel -Measure $measure -TargetDb -30

        $level.InBand | Should -BeTrue
        [math]::Abs($level.LevelDb + 30) | Should -BeLessOrEqual 3
        $level.Gain | Should -BeLessThan 0.5

        $quiet = Set-SpeakerLevel -Measure { param($Gain) -60 + 20 * [math]::Log10($Gain) } -TargetDb -30
        $quiet.InBand | Should -BeFalse
        $quiet.Gain | Should -Be 1

        (Set-SpeakerLevel -Measure { $null } -TargetDb -30).InBand | Should -BeFalse
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing tests of the readers of Android's dumps**

Create `scripts/tests/Oracle.Tests.ps1`:

```powershell
# Tests of scripts\lib\Oracle.ps1, which reads Android's dumps for the A71 sessions' automatic checks. The dumps are cut
# down from Android 13's formats. scripts\test-scripts.ps1 runs these tests in Windows PowerShell 5.1 and in PowerShell 7.
[Diagnostics.CodeAnalysis.SuppressMessageAttribute('PSUseDeclaredVarsMoreThanAssignments', '',
    Justification = 'Pester shares the variables of BeforeAll with the It blocks.')]
param()

BeforeAll {
    . "$PSScriptRoot\..\lib\Oracle.ps1"
}

Describe 'The readers of Android''s dumps' {
    It 'finds the package in front, the home app and whether the keyguard shows' {
        $activities = @(
            'Display #0 (activities from top to bottom):'
            '  * Task{6c1 #12 type=standard A=1000:com.android.settings}'
            '  topResumedActivity=ActivityRecord{9f3e2b1 u0 com.android.settings/.Settings t12}'
            'KeyguardController:'
            '  mKeyguardShowing=false'
        )

        Get-ResumedPackage $activities | Should -BeExactly 'com.android.settings'
        Get-ResumedPackage @('  mResumedActivity: ActivityRecord{1 u0 com.sec.android.app.launcher/.activities.LauncherActivity t1}') | Should -BeExactly 'com.sec.android.app.launcher'
        Get-ResumedPackage @('  topResumedActivity=null') | Should -BeNullOrEmpty
        Test-KeyguardShowing $activities | Should -BeFalse
        Test-KeyguardShowing @('  mKeyguardShowing=true') | Should -BeTrue
        Test-KeyguardShowing @() | Should -BeNullOrEmpty
        Get-HomePackage @('priority=0 preferredOrder=0 match=0x108000 specificIndex=-1 isDefault=true', 'com.sec.android.app.launcher/com.android.launcher3.uioverrides.QuickstepLauncher') | Should -BeExactly 'com.sec.android.app.launcher'
        Get-HomePackage @('No activity found') | Should -BeNullOrEmpty
    }

    It 'reads the power state, the keyboard and the focused window' {
        Get-Wakefulness @('POWER MANAGER (dumpsys power)', '  mWakefulness=Asleep', '  mWakefulnessChanging=false') | Should -BeExactly 'Asleep'
        Test-InputShown @('  mShowRequested=true mShowExplicitlyRequested=false', '  mInputShown=true') | Should -BeTrue
        Test-InputShown @('  mInputShown=false') | Should -BeFalse
        Test-InputShown @() | Should -BeNullOrEmpty
        Get-FocusedWindow @('  mCurrentFocus=Window{4a8 u0 com.android.settings/com.android.settings.Settings}') | Should -BeExactly 'com.android.settings'
        Get-FocusedWindow @('  mCurrentFocus=Window{77 u0 NotificationShade}') | Should -BeExactly 'NotificationShade'
        Get-FocusedWindow @('  mCurrentFocus=null') | Should -BeNullOrEmpty
    }

    It 'reads the playback state of one package''s media session, and the muted streams' {
        $sessions = @(
            '  Sessions Stack - have 2 sessions:'
            '    music 41 (userId=0)'
            '      ownerPid=1234, ownerUid=10200, userId=0'
            '      package=com.example.music'
            '      state=PlaybackState {state=3, position=1000, buffered position=0, speed=1.0}'
            '    fixture 42 (userId=0)'
            '      ownerPid=2345, ownerUid=10334, userId=0'
            '      package=dev.kestrel'
            '      state=PlaybackState {state=2, position=0, buffered position=0, speed=0.0}'
        )
        $audio = @(
            'Stream volumes (device: index)'
            '- STREAM_RING:'
            '   Muted: true'
            '- STREAM_MUSIC:'
            '   Muted: false'
            '   Muted Internally: false'
        )

        Get-PlaybackState -Dump $sessions -Package 'dev.kestrel' | Should -Be 2
        Get-PlaybackState -Dump $sessions -Package 'com.example.music' | Should -Be 3
        Get-PlaybackState -Dump $sessions -Package 'com.example.other' | Should -BeNullOrEmpty
        Get-PlaybackState -Dump @('    package=dev.kestrel', '      state=PlaybackState {state=PAUSED(2), position=0}') -Package 'dev.kestrel' | Should -Be 2
        Test-StreamMuted -Dump $audio | Should -BeFalse
        Test-StreamMuted -Dump $audio -Stream STREAM_RING | Should -BeTrue
        Test-StreamMuted -Dump $audio -Stream STREAM_ALARM | Should -BeNullOrEmpty
    }

    It 'hashes a text as the hooks do, and finds a fixed sentence among a command''s hashes' {
        Get-TextHash 'abc' | Should -BeExactly 'ba7816bf8f01cfea'
        $said = "1111111111111111,$(Get-TextHash 'Cancelled')"

        Test-HashedText -Hashes $said -Text 'Cancelled' | Should -BeTrue
        Test-HashedText -Hashes $said -Text 'cancelled' | Should -BeFalse
        Test-HashedText -Hashes 'none' -Text 'Cancelled' | Should -BeFalse
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the failing tests of the check runner**

Create `scripts/tests/AutoCheck.Tests.ps1`:

```powershell
# Tests of scripts\lib\AutoCheck.ps1, the check runner of the A71 sessions. The hooks are mocked, so no test needs a
# device. scripts\test-scripts.ps1 runs these tests in Windows PowerShell 5.1 and in PowerShell 7.
[Diagnostics.CodeAnalysis.SuppressMessageAttribute('PSUseDeclaredVarsMoreThanAssignments', '',
    Justification = 'Pester shares the variables of BeforeAll with the It blocks.')]
param()

BeforeAll {
    . "$PSScriptRoot\..\lib\Session.ps1"
    . "$PSScriptRoot\..\lib\AutoCheck.ps1"
    # The session scripts define this; the tests mock it.
    function Invoke-Hook([string]$Command, [hashtable]$With = @{}) { }

    # An answer of voice-state -Tag.
    function New-TaggedAnswer([string]$Tag, [string]$Role = 'command', [string]$Outcome = 'done', [string]$Phase = 'ended', [string]$Asking = 'false', [string]$Number = '7', [string]$Task = '0', [string]$Kind = 'Global') {
        "tag=$Tag role=$Role command=$Number task=$Task kind=$Kind outcome=$Outcome asking=$Asking phase=$Phase detail=none said=none shown=none read=other latest=7"
    }
}

Describe 'The check runner' {
    BeforeEach {
        Mock Start-Sleep { }
        $sent = [System.Collections.Generic.List[hashtable]]::new()
    }

    It 'sends the text with a new tag, waits for that tag''s command to end, and writes the test''s verdict' {
        $reads = @{ Count = 0 }
        Mock Invoke-Hook { $sent.Add($With); 'sent heard' } -ParameterFilter { $Command -eq 'voice' }
        Mock Invoke-Hook {
            $reads.Count++
            switch ($reads.Count) {
                1 { New-TaggedAnswer $With.Tag -Role pending -Outcome none -Phase pending }
                2 { New-TaggedAnswer $With.Tag -Outcome running -Phase running }
                default { New-TaggedAnswer $With.Tag }
            }
        } -ParameterFilter { $Command -eq 'voice-state' }

        $result = Invoke-AutoCheck -Name 'go home' -Text 'go home' -Before { 'before' } -Verify {
            param($State, $Before)
            [pscustomobject]@{ Passed = $State['outcome'] -eq 'done' -and $Before -eq 'before'; Detail = 'home in front' }
        }

        $reads.Count | Should -Be 3
        $sent.Count | Should -Be 1
        $sent[0].Text | Should -BeExactly 'go home'
        $sent[0].Tag | Should -Match '^k[0-9a-f]{12}$'
        $result.Tag | Should -BeExactly $sent[0].Tag
        $result.Passed | Should -BeTrue
        $result.State['task'] | Should -BeExactly '0'
        $result.Line | Should -BeExactly 'go home [text]: role=command kind=Global outcome=done check=pass (home in front)'
    }

    It 'answers the command''s question with its own tag, and waits until the command ends' {
        $device = @{ Phase = 'question'; Asking = 'true' }
        Mock Invoke-Hook {
            $sent.Add($With)
            if ($sent.Count -eq 2) { $device.Phase = 'ended'; $device.Asking = 'false' }
            'sent heard'
        } -ParameterFilter { $Command -eq 'voice' }
        Mock Invoke-Hook { New-TaggedAnswer $With.Tag -Outcome $(if ($device.Phase -eq 'ended') { 'done' } else { 'running' }) -Phase $device.Phase -Asking $device.Asking } -ParameterFilter { $Command -eq 'voice-state' }

        $result = Invoke-AutoCheck -Name 'tap Delete, then 1' -Text 'tap Delete' -Answer '1' -Verify { param($State) $State['outcome'] -eq 'done' }

        $sent.Count | Should -Be 2
        $sent[1].Text | Should -BeExactly '1'
        $sent[1].Tag | Should -Not -Be $sent[0].Tag
        $result.Line | Should -BeExactly 'tap Delete, then 1 [text]: role=command kind=Global outcome=done check=pass'
    }

    It 'plays a clip that the phone expects, and records the match''s counts without words' {
        $played = [System.Collections.Generic.List[string]]::new()
        $results = @{ Count = 0 }
        Mock Invoke-Hook { $sent.Add($With); "expecting tag=$($With.Tag) seconds=$($With.Seconds)" } -ParameterFilter { $Command -eq 'voice-expect' -and -not $With.Result }
        Mock Invoke-Hook {
            $results.Count++
            if ($results.Count -eq 1) { return "waiting tag=$($With.Tag)" }
            "tag=$($With.Tag) matched=true words=2 errors=0 stray=1 split=0 speechEnded=900 closed=1600 transcribed=2100 run=true command=7 kind=Global outcome=done right=-"
        } -ParameterFilter { $Command -eq 'voice-expect' -and $With.Result }
        Mock Invoke-Hook { New-TaggedAnswer $With.Tag } -ParameterFilter { $Command -eq 'voice-state' }
        $clip = [pscustomobject]@{ Voice = 'kokoro'; Text = 'Go home.'; Path = 'c004.wav' }

        $result = Invoke-AutoCheck -Name 'go home' -Text 'go home' -Clip $clip -Play { param($Clip) $played.Add($Clip.Path) } -Verify { $true }

        $sent[0].Text | Should -BeExactly 'Go home.'
        $played | Should -Be @('c004.wav')
        $result.Heard['transcribed'] | Should -Be '2100'
        $result.Line | Should -BeExactly 'go home [kokoro]: role=command kind=Global outcome=done check=pass matched=true words=2 errors=0 stray=1 split=0'
    }

    It 'fails a clip that the phone did not match, without running the test' {
        Mock Invoke-Hook { 'expecting' } -ParameterFilter { $Command -eq 'voice-expect' -and -not $With.Result }
        Mock Invoke-Hook { "tag=$($With.Tag) matched=false words=2 errors=2 stray=0 split=0 speechEnded=0 closed=0 transcribed=0 run=true command=0 kind=none outcome=none right=-" } -ParameterFilter { $Command -eq 'voice-expect' -and $With.Result }
        $clip = [pscustomobject]@{ Voice = 'piper'; Text = 'Go home.'; Path = 'c004.wav' }

        $result = Invoke-AutoCheck -Name 'go home' -Text 'go home' -Clip $clip -Verify { throw 'not called' }

        $result.Passed | Should -BeFalse
        $result.Line | Should -BeExactly 'go home [piper]: role=- kind=- outcome=- check=fail (the phone did not match the clip) matched=false words=2 errors=2 stray=0 split=0'
        Should -Invoke Invoke-Hook -Times 0 -Exactly -ParameterFilter { $Command -eq 'voice-state' }
    }

    It 'responds at the countdown after the delay, sends through the caller''s sender, and answers a question left open with no' {
        $device = @{ Phase = 'countdown'; Asking = 'false'; Tapped = 0 }
        $senders = [System.Collections.Generic.List[string]]::new()
        Mock Invoke-Hook {
            $sent.Add($With)
            if ($With.Text -eq 'no') { $device.Phase = 'ended'; $device.Asking = 'false' }
            'sent heard'
        } -ParameterFilter { $Command -eq 'voice' }
        Mock Invoke-Hook { New-TaggedAnswer $With.Tag -Outcome running -Phase $device.Phase -Asking $device.Asking } -ParameterFilter { $Command -eq 'voice-state' }

        $result = Invoke-AutoCheck -Name 'call Ana Test, then a tap' -Text 'call Ana Test' -Send { param($Tag) $senders.Add($Tag) } -RespondAt countdown -RespondDelay 3 -Respond {
            # The tap ends the countdown, and the command asks a question that the check did not plan.
            $device.Tapped++
            $device.Phase = 'question'
            $device.Asking = 'true'
        } -Verify { $true }

        $senders.Count | Should -Be 1
        $device.Tapped | Should -Be 1
        Should -Invoke Start-Sleep -Times 1 -Exactly -ParameterFilter { $Milliseconds -eq 3000 }
        $sent.Count | Should -Be 1
        $sent[0].Text | Should -BeExactly 'no'
        $result.Line | Should -BeExactly 'call Ana Test, then a tap [text]: role=command kind=Global outcome=running check=pass (it asked a question, which the check answered with no)'
    }
    It 'cancels a countdown that the check did not plan, or that a failed response left running, and fails the check' {
        $device = @{ Phase = 'countdown' }
        Mock Invoke-Hook {
            $sent.Add($With)
            if ($With.Text -eq 'cancel') { $device.Phase = 'ended' }
            'sent heard'
        } -ParameterFilter { $Command -eq 'voice' }
        Mock Invoke-Hook { New-TaggedAnswer $With.Tag -Outcome $(if ($device.Phase -eq 'ended') { 'cancelled' } else { 'running' }) -Phase $device.Phase } -ParameterFilter { $Command -eq 'voice-state' }

        $unplanned = Invoke-AutoCheck -Name 'open Ana' -Text 'open Ana' -Verify { $true }
        $device.Phase = 'countdown'
        $failedTap = Invoke-AutoCheck -Name 'call Ana Test, then a tap' -Text 'call Ana Test' -RespondAt countdown -Respond { throw 'The element has no bounds on the screen.' } -Verify { $true }

        @($sent | Where-Object { $_.Text -eq 'cancel' }).Count | Should -Be 2
        $unplanned.Passed | Should -BeFalse
        $unplanned.Line | Should -BeExactly 'open Ana [text]: role=command kind=Global outcome=cancelled check=fail (it counted down to a call, which the check cancelled)'
        $failedTap.Passed | Should -BeFalse
        $failedTap.Line | Should -Match 'the response failed \(The element has no bounds on the screen\.\); it counted down to a call, which the check cancelled'
    }

    It 'waits with the caller''s wait instead of the command''s end, such as a task''s result, for at most 10 minutes' {
        $polls = @{ Count = 0 }
        $waits = [System.Collections.Generic.List[double]]::new()
        Mock Invoke-Hook { $sent.Add($With); 'sent heard' } -ParameterFilter { $Command -eq 'voice' }
        Mock Invoke-Hook { New-TaggedAnswer $With.Tag -Role task -Number 0 -Task 3 -Kind none -Outcome none -Phase ended } -ParameterFilter { $Command -eq 'voice-state' }
        # A fake of sub-project 5's hook agent-task, which does not exist yet.
        Mock Invoke-Hook {
            $polls.Count++
            if ($polls.Count -lt 3) { "tag=$($With.Tag) state=running" } else { "tag=$($With.Tag) state=done" }
        } -ParameterFilter { $Command -eq 'agent-task' -and $With.Result }
        $wait = {
            param($Tag, $Seconds)
            $waits.Add($Seconds)
            do { $answer = ConvertFrom-HookPairs (Invoke-Hook agent-task @{ Result = $true; Tag = $Tag }) } while ($answer['state'] -eq 'running')
            $answer
        }

        $result = Invoke-AutoCheck -Name 'open the settings' -Text 'Kestrel, open the settings.' -Wait $wait -Verify {
            param($State, $Before, $Waited)
            $null -eq $Before -and $State['role'] -eq 'task' -and $State['task'] -eq '3' -and $Waited['state'] -eq 'done'
        }

        $polls.Count | Should -Be 3
        $waits | Should -Be @(600)
        $sent.Count | Should -Be 1
        $result.Waited['state'] | Should -BeExactly 'done'
        $result.Line | Should -BeExactly 'open the settings [text]: role=task kind=none outcome=none check=pass'
        Should -Invoke Invoke-Hook -Times 1 -Exactly -ParameterFilter { $Command -eq 'voice-state' }
        { Invoke-AutoCheck -Name 'x' -Text 'x' -Wait $wait -WaitSeconds 601 -Verify { $true } } | Should -Throw
        { Invoke-AutoCheck -Name 'x' -Text 'x' -Wait $wait -Answer '1' -Verify { $true } } | Should -Throw '*cannot answer or respond*'
    }

    It 'never sends "yes, call", in any form' {
        Mock Invoke-Hook { }

        { Invoke-AutoCheck -Name 'x' -Text 'Yes, call.' -Verify { $true } } | Should -Throw '*process rule 10*'
        { Invoke-AutoCheck -Name 'x' -Text 'tap call' -Answer 'yes call Kestrel' -Verify { $true } } | Should -Throw '*process rule 10*'
        Test-YesCall 'no' | Should -BeFalse
        Should -Invoke Invoke-Hook -Times 0 -Exactly
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the failing tests of the owner's settings and the test PIN**

In `scripts/tests/Session.Tests.ps1`, add at the end of the file:

```powershell
Describe 'The owner''s settings and the test PIN' {
    BeforeAll {
        . "$PSScriptRoot\..\lib\Session.ps1"
    }

    It 'reads the owner''s settings for the A71 sessions, with defaults for a missing file or field, and refuses a bad number' {
        $path = Join-Path $TestDrive 'a71-config.json'
        Set-Content -LiteralPath $path -Value '{"callNumber": "064 123-4567", "testContacts": true, "speakerTargetDb": -30, "pin": "2468", "later": {"plan": 5}}'

        $config = Read-A71Config $path

        $config.CallNumber | Should -BeExactly '0641234567'
        $config.TestContacts | Should -BeTrue
        $config.SpeakerTargetDb | Should -Be -30
        ($config | Out-String) | Should -Not -Match '2468'
        $config.PSObject.Properties.Name | Should -Not -Contain 'pin'
        $config.later.plan | Should -Be 5
        $missing = Read-A71Config (Join-Path $TestDrive 'none.json')
        $missing.CallNumber | Should -BeNullOrEmpty
        $missing.TestContacts | Should -BeFalse
        $missing.SpeakerTargetDb | Should -Be -26
        Set-Content -LiteralPath $path -Value '{"callNumber": "112"}'
        { Read-A71Config $path } | Should -Throw '*callNumber must have 5 to 15 digits.'
    }

    It 'checks the A71''s test PIN, and says what is missing without the PIN' {
        $path = Join-Path $TestDrive 'a71-config.json'
        Set-Content -LiteralPath $path -Value '{"pin": "024680", "callNumber": "0641234567"}'

        Send-TestPin -ConfigPath $path -CheckOnly | Should -BeNullOrEmpty
        { Send-TestPin -ConfigPath (Join-Path $TestDrive 'none.json') -CheckOnly } | Should -Throw '*test PIN is missing, because*does not exist*key "pin"*'
        Set-Content -LiteralPath $path -Value '{"callNumber": "0641234567"}'
        { Send-TestPin -ConfigPath $path -CheckOnly } | Should -Throw '*has no key "pin"*'
        Set-Content -LiteralPath $path -Value '{"pin": "12ab"}'
        $message = try { Send-TestPin -Serial 'R58N' -ConfigPath $path } catch { $_.Exception.Message }
        $message | Should -Match 'must be 4 to 16 digits'
        $message | Should -Not -Match '12ab'
        # A number: PowerShell 7 refuses the leading zero, and Windows PowerShell 5.1 would read another PIN.
        foreach ($text in '{"pin": 024680}', '{"pin": 2468}') {
            Set-Content -LiteralPath $path -Value $text
            $message = try { Send-TestPin -ConfigPath $path -CheckOnly } catch { $_.Exception.Message }
            $message | Should -Match 'quotes'
            $message | Should -Not -Match '2468'
        }
    }

    Context 'The unlock with the test PIN' {
        BeforeAll {
            . "$PSScriptRoot\..\lib\Kestrel.ps1"
            . "$PSScriptRoot\..\lib\Oracle.ps1"
            # A phone that Assert-TestDevice accepted, without asking a device.
            $KestrelTestDevices['R58N'] = [pscustomobject]@{ Property = 'ro.product.model'; Pattern = 'SM-A715F*' }
            # A fake adb that fails and repeats its whole command in its error, as adb can.
            $fake = Join-Path $TestDrive 'failing-adb.ps1'
            Set-Content -LiteralPath $fake -Value '[Console]::Error.WriteLine("error: closed while running: $($args -join '' '')"); exit 1'
            $fakeAdb = Join-Path $TestDrive 'failing-adb.cmd'
            Set-Content -LiteralPath $fakeAdb -Value "@echo off`r`npowershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File ""$fake"" %*`r`nexit /b %ERRORLEVEL%"
            # The owner's configuration with the test PIN.
            $config = Join-Path $TestDrive 'pin-config.json'
            Set-Content -LiteralPath $config -Value '{"pin": "2468"}'
        }

        BeforeEach {
            Mock Start-Sleep { }
            $script:TestPinRefused = $false
            # Keyguard: 'true', 'false' or 'unknown' (no line in the dump); Occluded: whether an activity shows over it;
            # Pad: whether the PIN pad's entry shows.
            $phone = @{ Keyguard = 'true'; Occluded = 'false'; Pad = $true; Typed = 0; Commands = [System.Collections.Generic.List[string]]::new() }
            Mock Invoke-SettingsCommand {
                $phone.Commands.Add($Command)
                if ($Command -eq 'dumpsys activity activities') {
                    if ($phone.Keyguard -ne 'unknown') { "  mKeyguardShowing=$($phone.Keyguard)" } else { '  (no keyguard line)' }
                    "    Occluded=$($phone.Occluded) DismissingKeyguardActivity=null at display=0"
                }
                if ($Command -like 'uiautomator dump*') { if ($phone.Pad) { '1' } else { '0' } }
                if ($Command -eq 'wm size') { 'Physical size: 1080x2400' }
            }
        }

        It 'types the PIN once, only while the lock screen''s PIN pad shows' {
            Mock Send-TestPin { if (-not $CheckOnly) { $phone.Typed++; $phone.Keyguard = 'false' } }

            Unlock-TestPhone -Serial 'R58N' -ConfigPath $config | Should -BeTrue

            $phone.Typed | Should -Be 1
            @($phone.Commands | Where-Object { $_ -match '2468' }).Count | Should -Be 0
            $phone.Commands.IndexOf('wm dismiss-keyguard') | Should -BeGreaterThan -1
            # One look at the screen's hierarchy, in the shell's folder, which exists before the first unlock.
            @($phone.Commands | Where-Object { $_ -like 'uiautomator dump *' }).Count | Should -Be 1
            @($phone.Commands | Where-Object { $_ -like 'uiautomator dump /data/local/tmp/*' }).Count | Should -Be 1
            Unlock-TestPhone -Serial 'R58N' -ConfigPath $config | Should -BeTrue
            $phone.Typed | Should -Be 1
        }

        It 'types nothing when Android does not say clearly that the PIN pad shows, or an activity shows over the lock screen' {
            Mock Send-TestPin { if (-not $CheckOnly) { $phone.Typed++ } }
            $phone.Keyguard = 'unknown'

            Unlock-TestPhone -Serial 'R58N' -ConfigPath $config | Should -BeFalse
            $phone.Keyguard = 'true'
            $phone.Occluded = 'true'
            Unlock-TestPhone -Serial 'R58N' -ConfigPath $config | Should -BeFalse
            $phone.Occluded = 'false'
            $phone.Pad = $false
            Unlock-TestPhone -Serial 'R58N' -ConfigPath $config | Should -BeFalse

            $phone.Typed | Should -Be 0
            @($phone.Commands | Where-Object { $_ -like 'input swipe *' }).Count | Should -Be 1
        }

        It 'types a PIN that did not unlock the phone only once in the run' {
            Mock Send-TestPin { if (-not $CheckOnly) { $phone.Typed++ } }

            { Unlock-TestPhone -Serial 'R58N' -ConfigPath $config } | Should -Throw 'the unlock failed'
            { Unlock-TestPhone -Serial 'R58N' -ConfigPath $config } | Should -Throw '*earlier in this run*'

            $phone.Typed | Should -Be 1
        }

        It 'says only that the unlock failed when adb fails, and no output, warning or error holds the PIN' {
            Mock Get-Adb { $fakeAdb }

            $output = & {
                try { Unlock-TestPhone -Serial 'R58N' -ConfigPath $config } catch { $_; $_.Exception.Message; $_.ScriptStackTrace }
                try { Unlock-TestPhone -Serial 'R58N' -ConfigPath $config } catch { $_; $_.Exception.Message }
            } *>&1 | Out-String

            $output | Should -Match 'the unlock failed'
            $output | Should -Not -Match '2468'
        }
    }
}
```

In both shells, Pester reports N passed and 23 failed: the 5 tests of `Speaker.Tests.ps1`, the 4 of `Oracle.Tests.ps1` and the 8 of `AutoCheck.Tests.ps1` fail in their `BeforeAll`, because the three libraries do not exist; in `Session.Tests.ps1`, `reads the owner's settings for the A71 sessions…` and `checks the A71's test PIN…` fail with "The term 'Read-A71Config' is not recognized" and "The term 'Send-TestPin' is not recognized", and the four tests of `The unlock with the test PIN` fail in their `BeforeAll`, because `Oracle.ps1` does not exist.

- [ ] **Step 6: Write the speaker**

Create `scripts/lib/Speaker.ps1`:

```powershell
# Helpers for the speech that this computer plays to the Galaxy A71 through its default speakers (plan 4D): reading and
# writing the corpus's WAV clips, where the speech of a clip ends, a gain on the samples that sets the loudness at the
# phone, playing a clip, and the offset between this computer's clock and the phone's. Dot-source scripts\lib\Session.ps1
# first: Get-PhoneClock uses ConvertFrom-Uptime. Nothing here reads or prints what Kestrel heard.

# The loops over the samples, which would take minutes in PowerShell for the 30-minute room-sound track.
if (-not ('KestrelSpeech' -as [type])) {
    Add-Type -TypeDefinition @'
public static class KestrelSpeech {
    // The 16-bit samples multiplied by the gain, kept within the 16-bit range.
    public static byte[] Scale(byte[] samples, double gain) {
        byte[] scaled = new byte[samples.Length];
        for (int i = 0; i + 1 < samples.Length; i += 2) {
            double value = System.Math.Round((short)(samples[i] | (samples[i + 1] << 8)) * gain);
            short clipped = (short)System.Math.Max(-32768, System.Math.Min(32767, value));
            scaled[i] = (byte)(clipped & 0xff);
            scaled[i + 1] = (byte)((clipped >> 8) & 0xff);
        }
        return scaled;
    }

    // The byte offset just after the last 16-bit sample whose size is at least the limit, or 0 without one.
    public static int EndOfLoud(byte[] samples, double limit) {
        for (int i = samples.Length - 2; i >= 0; i -= 2) {
            int value = (short)(samples[i] | (samples[i + 1] << 8));
            if (System.Math.Abs(value) >= limit) return i + 2;
        }
        return 0;
    }
}
'@
}

# This computer's clock for the speech, in milliseconds since an arbitrary start: the stopwatch's counter, which every
# PowerShell process reads the same.
function Get-PcMilliseconds { [double][Diagnostics.Stopwatch]::GetTimestamp() * 1000 / [Diagnostics.Stopwatch]::Frequency }

# Reads a 16-bit PCM WAV file, such as a clip of the corpus (16 kHz, mono): its sample rate, its channels and the bytes of
# its samples. Throws for any other file.
function Read-WavFile([string]$Path) {
    $bytes = [IO.File]::ReadAllBytes($Path)
    $ascii = [Text.Encoding]::ASCII
    if ($bytes.Length -lt 12 -or $ascii.GetString($bytes, 0, 4) -ne 'RIFF' -or $ascii.GetString($bytes, 8, 4) -ne 'WAVE') {
        throw "Not a WAV file: $Path"
    }
    $at = 12
    $format = $null
    while ($at + 8 -le $bytes.Length) {
        $id = $ascii.GetString($bytes, $at, 4)
        $size = [BitConverter]::ToInt32($bytes, $at + 4)
        if ($id -eq 'fmt ') {
            $format = [pscustomobject]@{
                Code = [BitConverter]::ToInt16($bytes, $at + 8)
                Channels = [int][BitConverter]::ToInt16($bytes, $at + 10)
                SampleRate = [BitConverter]::ToInt32($bytes, $at + 12)
                Bits = [BitConverter]::ToInt16($bytes, $at + 22)
            }
        } elseif ($id -eq 'data') {
            if (-not $format -or $format.Code -ne 1 -or $format.Bits -ne 16) { throw "Not a 16-bit PCM WAV file: $Path" }
            $length = [math]::Min($size, $bytes.Length - $at - 8)
            $samples = New-Object byte[] $length
            [Array]::Copy($bytes, $at + 8, $samples, 0, $length)
            return [pscustomobject]@{ SampleRate = $format.SampleRate; Channels = $format.Channels; Samples = $samples }
        }
        $at += 8 + $size + ($size % 2)
    }
    throw "Not a 16-bit PCM WAV file: $Path"
}

# The bytes of a 16-bit PCM WAV file with these samples.
function ConvertTo-WavBytes([byte[]]$Samples, [int]$SampleRate, [int]$Channels = 1) {
    $stream = New-Object IO.MemoryStream
    $writer = New-Object IO.BinaryWriter($stream)
    $ascii = [Text.Encoding]::ASCII
    $writer.Write($ascii.GetBytes('RIFF'))
    $writer.Write([int](36 + $Samples.Length))
    $writer.Write($ascii.GetBytes('WAVEfmt '))
    $writer.Write([int]16)
    $writer.Write([int16]1)
    $writer.Write([int16]$Channels)
    $writer.Write([int]$SampleRate)
    $writer.Write([int]($SampleRate * $Channels * 2))
    $writer.Write([int16]($Channels * 2))
    $writer.Write([int16]16)
    $writer.Write($ascii.GetBytes('data'))
    $writer.Write([int]$Samples.Length)
    $writer.Write($Samples)
    $writer.Flush()
    , $stream.ToArray()
}

# Writes a 16-bit PCM WAV file with these samples.
function Write-WavFile([string]$Path, [byte[]]$Samples, [int]$SampleRate, [int]$Channels = 1) {
    [IO.File]::WriteAllBytes($Path, (ConvertTo-WavBytes -Samples $Samples -SampleRate $SampleRate -Channels $Channels))
}

# Where the speech of a clip ends: the end of its last sample that is at least $ThresholdDb loud (-40 dBFS by default), in
# milliseconds from the clip's start, and the clip's length, so that the silence after the speech is known. A clip
# without such a sample has its speech end at 0.
function Get-ClipSpeechEnd([string]$Path, [double]$ThresholdDb = -40) {
    $wav = Read-WavFile $Path
    $bytesPerMs = $wav.SampleRate * $wav.Channels * 2 / 1000.0
    $end = [KestrelSpeech]::EndOfLoud($wav.Samples, 32768 * [math]::Pow(10, $ThresholdDb / 20))
    [pscustomobject]@{
        SpeechEndMs = [math]::Round($end / $bytesPerMs)
        LengthMs = [math]::Round($wav.Samples.Length / $bytesPerMs)
    }
}

# A clip's file as WAV bytes with its samples multiplied by $Gain, ready for System.Media.SoundPlayer.
function Get-ScaledClip([string]$Path, [double]$Gain) {
    $wav = Read-WavFile $Path
    , (ConvertTo-WavBytes -Samples ([KestrelSpeech]::Scale($wav.Samples, $Gain)) -SampleRate $wav.SampleRate -Channels $wav.Channels)
}

# Plays a clip through this computer's default speakers with the gain, and returns when it started and when it ended on
# the clock of Get-PcMilliseconds. PlaySync returns when the sound has ended; Load reads the whole clip first, so that the
# start is not delayed.
function Invoke-Clip([string]$Path, [double]$Gain = 1) {
    $stream = New-Object IO.MemoryStream(, (Get-ScaledClip -Path $Path -Gain $Gain))
    $player = New-Object System.Media.SoundPlayer($stream)
    try {
        $player.Load()
        $start = Get-PcMilliseconds
        $player.PlaySync()
        $end = Get-PcMilliseconds
    } finally {
        $player.Dispose()
        $stream.Dispose()
    }
    [pscustomobject]@{ StartMs = $start; EndMs = $end }
}

# Starts playing a clip over and over through this computer's default speakers, such as the room-sound track, and returns
# the player for Stop-ClipLoop.
function Start-ClipLoop([string]$Path, [double]$Gain = 1) {
    $player = New-Object System.Media.SoundPlayer((New-Object IO.MemoryStream(, (Get-ScaledClip -Path $Path -Gain $Gain))))
    $player.Load()
    $player.PlayLooping()
    $player
}

# Stops the player of Start-ClipLoop.
function Stop-ClipLoop($Player) {
    if (-not $Player) { return }
    $Player.Stop()
    $Player.Stream.Dispose()
    $Player.Dispose()
}

# The offset between this computer's clock (Get-PcMilliseconds) and the phone's clock of Kestrel's times
# (SystemClock.elapsedRealtime, which /proc/uptime counts too): $ReadUptime reads the phone's answer of 'cat /proc/uptime'
# once. Of $Reads reads, the one with the shortest round trip counts: the offset is the phone's time minus the middle of
# its round trip, and the error is half the round trip plus the 10 ms steps of /proc/uptime. A time T on this computer is
# T + OffsetMs on the phone. Read it again every 10 clips, because the clocks drift apart a little.
function Get-PhoneClock([scriptblock]$ReadUptime, [int]$Reads = 10) {
    $best = $null
    for ($i = 0; $i -lt $Reads; $i++) {
        $before = Get-PcMilliseconds
        $phone = ConvertFrom-Uptime "$(& $ReadUptime)"
        $after = Get-PcMilliseconds
        if (-not $best -or $after - $before -lt $best.Trip) {
            $best = [pscustomobject]@{ Trip = $after - $before; Offset = $phone - ($before + $after) / 2 }
        }
    }
    [pscustomobject]@{ OffsetMs = [long][math]::Round($best.Offset); ErrorMs = [long][math]::Round($best.Trip / 2 + 10) }
}

# Finds the gain at which the phone hears this computer's clips at $TargetDb, the RMS level in dBFS that Kestrel's
# microphone measured, within $Band dB. $Measure plays the calibration clip with a gain and returns the level that the
# phone measured, or $null when it measured nothing. It starts at $Start and corrects the gain by the difference in dB, at
# most $Tries times; the gain stays at most 1, because a larger gain would clip the samples. Returns the gain, the last
# level and whether it was within the band. Below the band at gain 1, this computer's own volume is too low.
function Set-SpeakerLevel([scriptblock]$Measure, [double]$TargetDb, [double]$Band = 3, [double]$Start = 0.5, [int]$Tries = 6) {
    $gain = $Start
    $level = $null
    for ($try = 1; $try -le $Tries; $try++) {
        $level = & $Measure $gain
        if ($null -eq $level) { break }
        $level = [double]$level
        if ([math]::Abs($level - $TargetDb) -le $Band) { return [pscustomobject]@{ Gain = $gain; LevelDb = $level; InBand = $true } }
        if ($level -lt $TargetDb -and $gain -ge 1) { break }
        $gain = [math]::Min([double]1, [math]::Max([double]0.01, $gain * [math]::Pow(10, ($TargetDb - $level) / 20)))
    }
    [pscustomobject]@{ Gain = $gain; LevelDb = $level; InBand = $false }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 7: Write the readers of Android's dumps**

Create `scripts/lib/Oracle.ps1`:

```powershell
# Readers of Android's dumps for the A71 sessions' automatic checks (plan 4D): which app is in front, whether the phone is
# awake or locked, whether the keyboard shows, the playback state of a media session, the muted streams, the window that
# has the focus and the home app. Each keeps only a package name, a state or a number from the dump, never other text.

# The package of the activity in front, from 'dumpsys activity activities' ('topResumedActivity=ActivityRecord{1a2b u0
# com.android.settings/.Settings t12}'), or $null when no activity is resumed.
function Get-ResumedPackage([string[]]$Dump) {
    foreach ($line in $Dump) {
        if ($line -match '\btopResumedActivity=ActivityRecord\{\S+ u\d+ ([\w.]+)/') { return $Matches[1] }
    }
    foreach ($line in $Dump) {
        if ($line -match '\bmResumedActivity: ActivityRecord\{\S+ u\d+ ([\w.]+)/') { return $Matches[1] }
    }
    $null
}

# The package of the home app, from 'cmd package resolve-activity --brief -a android.intent.action.MAIN -c
# android.intent.category.HOME', whose last line is the component, such as
# 'com.sec.android.app.launcher/com.android.launcher3.uioverrides.QuickstepLauncher', or $null.
function Get-HomePackage([string[]]$Answer) {
    $component = @($Answer | Where-Object { "$_".Trim() -match '^[\w.]+/[\w.$]+$' }) | Select-Object -Last 1
    if (-not $component) { return $null }
    ("$component".Trim() -split '/')[0]
}

# The power state, such as 'Awake' or 'Asleep', from 'dumpsys power' ('mWakefulness=Awake'), or $null.
function Get-Wakefulness([string[]]$Dump) {
    foreach ($line in $Dump) {
        if ($line -match '^\s*mWakefulness=(\w+)') { return $Matches[1] }
    }
    $null
}

# Whether the keyguard shows, from 'dumpsys activity activities' (its KeyguardController's 'mKeyguardShowing=true'), or
# $null when the dump does not say.
function Test-KeyguardShowing([string[]]$Dump) {
    foreach ($line in $Dump) {
        if ($line -match '\bmKeyguardShowing=(true|false)\b') { return $Matches[1] -eq 'true' }
    }
    $null
}

# Whether the keyboard shows, from 'dumpsys input_method' ('mInputShown=true'), or $null when the dump does not say.
function Test-InputShown([string[]]$Dump) {
    foreach ($line in $Dump) {
        if ($line -match '\bmInputShown=(true|false)\b') { return $Matches[1] -eq 'true' }
    }
    $null
}

# The playback state of the package's media session, from 'dumpsys media_session': the number in its line
# 'state=PlaybackState {state=3, ...' (2 paused, 3 playing), as Android 13 prints it, or 'state=PLAYING(3)' as Android 14
# and later print it, or $null when the package has no session. A session's lines follow its line 'package=<package>'.
function Get-PlaybackState([string[]]$Dump, [string]$Package) {
    $current = $null
    foreach ($line in $Dump) {
        if ($line -match '^\s*package=(\S+)\s*$') { $current = $Matches[1] }
        if ($current -eq $Package -and $line -match '\bstate=PlaybackState \{state=(?:[A-Z_]+\()?(\d+)') { return [int]$Matches[1] }
    }
    $null
}

# Whether a stream is muted, from 'dumpsys audio', whose list of stream volumes has a line '- STREAM_MUSIC:' and then
# '   Muted: true', or $null when the dump does not say.
function Test-StreamMuted([string[]]$Dump, [string]$Stream = 'STREAM_MUSIC') {
    $inStream = $false
    foreach ($line in $Dump) {
        if ($line -match '^- (STREAM_\w+):') {
            $inStream = $Matches[1] -eq $Stream
            continue
        }
        if ($inStream -and $line -match '^\s+Muted: (true|false)\b') { return $Matches[1] -eq 'true' }
    }
    $null
}

# The package or window name of the window that has the focus, from 'dumpsys window' ('mCurrentFocus=Window{4a8 u0
# com.android.settings/com.android.settings.Settings}'), such as 'com.android.settings', or the window's title when it
# names no package, or $null.
function Get-FocusedWindow([string[]]$Dump) {
    foreach ($line in $Dump) {
        if ($line -match '\bmCurrentFocus=Window\{\S+ u\d+ ([^/}\s]+)') { return $Matches[1] }
    }
    $null
}

# The hash that the hooks give for a text instead of the text: the first 16 hexadecimal characters of the SHA-256 of its
# UTF-8 bytes (plan 4D interfaces, section 1), so that a check compares what Kestrel said or showed with a fixed
# sentence without the words leaving the phone.
function Get-TextHash([string]$Text) {
    $sha = [Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::Create()
    try { $bytes = $sha.ComputeHash([Text.UTF8Encoding]::new($false).GetBytes($Text)) } finally { $sha.Dispose() }
    -join ($bytes[0..7] | ForEach-Object { $_.ToString('x2') })
}

# Whether the hashes of a 'said=' or 'shown=' field of voice-state -Tag, separated by commas, hold the hash of $Text.
function Test-HashedText([string]$Hashes, [string]$Text) {
    @("$Hashes" -split ',') -contains (Get-TextHash $Text)
}
```

- [ ] **Step 8: Write the check runner**

Create `scripts/lib/AutoCheck.ps1`:

```powershell
# The check runner of the A71 sessions' automatic checks (plan 4D): it gives Kestrel a command as a tagged transcript
# (the text path, hook H1) or plays the command's clip through this computer's speakers and lets the phone match it to
# the expected text (the air path, hook H2), waits for the result of exactly that command by its tag, never for
# Kestrel's newest command, runs the check's own test of the device, and writes one line. Dot-source
# scripts\lib\Session.ps1 first; the caller defines Invoke-Hook([string]$Command, [hashtable]$With), as the session
# scripts do. Nothing here holds what Kestrel heard: the hooks answer only kinds, counts, times and hashes.

# A new tag for a transcript: 'k' and 12 hexadecimal characters, which hook.ps1 accepts ('^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,32}$').
function New-CheckTag { 'k' + [guid]::NewGuid().ToString('N').Substring(0, 12) }

# Whether a text is "yes, call" (process rule 10), which no check ever sends; the hook refuses it on a phone too.
function Test-YesCall([string]$Text) {
    ("$Text".ToLowerInvariant() -replace '[^a-z ]', ' ' -replace '\s+', ' ').Trim() -match '^(kestrel )?yes call( kestrel)?$'
}

# Reads an answer of 'voice-state -Tag', such as 'tag=k1 role=command command=7 task=0 kind=Scroll outcome=done
# asking=false phase=ended detail=none said=none shown=none read=other latest=7', into an ordered table. A transcript that
# started a task has 'role=task', 'command=0' and its task's number in 'task'; an answer without 'task' gets 'task=0'.
function ConvertFrom-TaggedState([string]$Text) {
    $state = ConvertFrom-HookPairs $Text
    foreach ($name in 'tag', 'role', 'command', 'kind', 'outcome', 'asking', 'phase') {
        if (-not $state.Contains($name)) { throw "Not an answer of voice-state -Tag: '$Text'" }
    }
    if (-not $state.Contains('task')) { $state['task'] = '0' }
    $state
}

# Whether the tagged transcript's command has settled: Kestrel handled the transcript, and its command ended, asks a
# question, or never started.
function Test-TaggedSettled($State) {
    $State['role'] -ne 'pending' -and $State['phase'] -in 'none', 'ended', 'question'
}

# Reads 'voice-state -Tag' until $Until is true for the state, at most $TimeoutSeconds, and returns the last state.
function Wait-TaggedState([string]$Tag, [scriptblock]$Until = { param($State) Test-TaggedSettled $State }, [double]$TimeoutSeconds = 15) {
    $watch = [Diagnostics.Stopwatch]::StartNew()
    while ($true) {
        $state = ConvertFrom-TaggedState (Invoke-Hook voice-state @{ Tag = $Tag })
        if ((& $Until $state) -or $watch.Elapsed.TotalSeconds -ge $TimeoutSeconds) { return $state }
        Start-Sleep -Milliseconds 250
    }
}

# Reads 'voice-expect -Result -Tag' until the expectation has ended, at most $TimeoutSeconds, and returns its answer as
# an ordered table, or $null while it still waits.
function Wait-ExpectResult([string]$Tag, [double]$TimeoutSeconds = 30) {
    $watch = [Diagnostics.Stopwatch]::StartNew()
    while ($true) {
        $text = "$(Invoke-Hook voice-expect @{ Result = $true; Tag = $Tag })".Trim()
        if ($text -notlike 'waiting*') { return ConvertFrom-HookPairs $text }
        if ($watch.Elapsed.TotalSeconds -ge $TimeoutSeconds) { return $null }
        Start-Sleep -Milliseconds 250
    }
}

# A check's verdict from what its test returned: $true, $false, or an object with Passed and Detail, where Detail names
# only states and numbers of the device, never screen text.
function ConvertTo-Verdict($Result) {
    if ($Result -is [bool]) { return [pscustomobject]@{ Passed = $Result; Detail = '' } }
    if ($null -eq $Result) { return [pscustomobject]@{ Passed = $false; Detail = 'no test result' } }
    $detail = if ($Result.Detail) { "$($Result.Detail)" } else { '' }
    [pscustomobject]@{ Passed = [bool]$Result.Passed; Detail = $detail }
}

# One automatic check. $Before reads the device before the command, and its result goes to $Verify as its second
# argument; $Verify gets the tagged state (voice-state -Tag) and returns the verdict (ConvertTo-Verdict). Without $Clip
# the text path sends $Text as a tagged transcript, or $Send sends it with the tag as its argument (as screen-has
# -Template does); with $Clip ({ Voice, Text, Path }) the air path asks the phone to expect the clip's text
# (voice-expect) and $Play plays the clip. Once the command asks its question, or with -RespondAt countdown once it counts
# down, and $RespondDelay seconds later, $Answer is sent as a tagged transcript, or $Respond runs, such as a tap on the
# screen. A question that is still open at the end is answered "no", so that it never takes the next check's words; the
# line says so. A countdown to a call that the check did not plan, or one that goes on after a response that failed, gets
# "cancel" at once, before Kestrel can press Call, and the check fails; the line says so. With $Wait, such as a script block that polls sub-project 5's 'agent-task -Result -Tag' until a task
# ends, the runner calls $Wait with the tag and $WaitSeconds (at most 600) instead of waiting for the command's end,
# reads the tagged state once after it, and gives $Verify what $Wait returned as its third argument; $Wait cannot be
# combined with $Answer or $Respond. Returns the result with the line for the results file.
function Invoke-AutoCheck(
    [string]$Name,
    [string]$Text,
    [scriptblock]$Verify,
    [scriptblock]$Before = { $null },
    [string]$Answer = '',
    [scriptblock]$Respond = $null,
    [ValidateSet('question', 'countdown')][string]$RespondAt = 'question',
    [double]$RespondDelay = 0,
    [scriptblock]$Send = $null,
    $Clip = $null,
    [scriptblock]$Play = { },
    [double]$TimeoutSeconds = 15,
    [scriptblock]$Wait = $null,
    [ValidateRange(1, 600)][double]$WaitSeconds = 600
) {
    if ((Test-YesCall $Text) -or (Test-YesCall $Answer) -or ($Clip -and (Test-YesCall $Clip.Text))) {
        throw 'No check may say "yes, call" (process rule 10).'
    }
    if ($Wait -and ($Answer -or $Respond)) { throw 'A check with -Wait cannot answer or respond (-Answer, -Respond).' }
    $context = & $Before
    $tag = New-CheckTag
    $heard = $null
    if ($Clip) {
        Invoke-Hook voice-expect @{ Text = $Clip.Text; Tag = $tag; Seconds = [int][math]::Ceiling($TimeoutSeconds + 5) } | Out-Null
        # A clip that could not play lets its expectation end first, so that it does not drop the next check's words.
        try { & $Play $Clip } catch { Wait-ExpectResult -Tag $tag -TimeoutSeconds ($TimeoutSeconds + 10) | Out-Null; throw }
        $heard = Wait-ExpectResult -Tag $tag -TimeoutSeconds ($TimeoutSeconds + 10)
    } elseif ($Send) {
        & $Send $tag
    } else {
        Invoke-Hook voice @{ Send = 'heard'; Text = $Text; Tag = $tag } | Out-Null
    }
    $state = $null
    $waited = $null
    $notes = @()
    $failed = $false
    if (-not $Clip -or ($heard -and $heard['matched'] -eq 'true')) {
        $responds = $Answer -or $Respond
        if ($Wait) {
            $waited = & $Wait $tag $WaitSeconds
            $state = ConvertFrom-TaggedState (Invoke-Hook voice-state @{ Tag = $tag })
        } else {
            $until = if ($responds -and $RespondAt -eq 'countdown') {
                { param($State) $State['phase'] -eq 'countdown' -or ($State['role'] -ne 'pending' -and $State['phase'] -in 'none', 'ended') }
            } else {
                # A countdown ends the wait too, so that one that the check did not plan is cancelled in time.
                { param($State) $State['phase'] -eq 'countdown' -or (Test-TaggedSettled $State) }
            }
            $state = Wait-TaggedState -Tag $tag -Until $until -TimeoutSeconds $TimeoutSeconds
        }
        $ready = if ($RespondAt -eq 'countdown') { $state['phase'] -eq 'countdown' } else { $state['asking'] -eq 'true' }
        if ($responds -and $ready) {
            if ($RespondDelay -gt 0) { Start-Sleep -Milliseconds ([int]($RespondDelay * 1000)) }
            try {
                if ($Respond) { & $Respond } else { Invoke-Hook voice @{ Send = 'heard'; Text = $Answer; Tag = (New-CheckTag) } | Out-Null }
            } catch {
                $failed = $true
                $notes += "the response failed ($(("$($_.Exception.Message)" -split "`r?`n")[0]))"
            }
            $state = if ($failed) {
                ConvertFrom-TaggedState (Invoke-Hook voice-state @{ Tag = $tag })
            } else {
                # Settled again also when the command asks another question, which the "no" below answers.
                Wait-TaggedState -Tag $tag -Until { param($State) $State['phase'] -ne 'countdown' -and (Test-TaggedSettled $State) } -TimeoutSeconds $TimeoutSeconds
            }
        }
        if ($state['phase'] -eq 'countdown' -and ($failed -or -not ($responds -and $RespondAt -eq 'countdown'))) {
            Invoke-Hook voice @{ Send = 'heard'; Text = 'cancel'; Tag = (New-CheckTag) } | Out-Null
            $state = Wait-TaggedState -Tag $tag -Until { param($State) $State['phase'] -in 'none', 'ended', 'question' } -TimeoutSeconds $TimeoutSeconds
            $notes += 'it counted down to a call, which the check cancelled'
            $failed = $true
        }
        if ($state['asking'] -eq 'true') {
            Invoke-Hook voice @{ Send = 'heard'; Text = 'no'; Tag = (New-CheckTag) } | Out-Null
            $state = Wait-TaggedState -Tag $tag -Until { param($State) $State['phase'] -in 'none', 'ended' } -TimeoutSeconds $TimeoutSeconds
            $notes += 'it asked a question, which the check answered with no'
        }
    }
    $verdict = if ($state) { ConvertTo-Verdict (& $Verify $state $context $waited) } else { [pscustomobject]@{ Passed = $false; Detail = 'the phone did not match the clip' } }
    if ($verdict.Detail) { $notes = @($verdict.Detail) + $notes }
    if ($failed) { $verdict.Passed = $false }
    $path = if ($Clip) { $Clip.Voice } else { 'text' }
    $command = if ($state) { "role=$($state['role']) kind=$($state['kind']) outcome=$($state['outcome'])" } else { 'role=- kind=- outcome=-' }
    $line = "$Name [$path]: $command check=$(if ($verdict.Passed) { 'pass' } else { 'fail' })"
    if ($notes) { $line += " ($($notes -join '; '))" }
    if ($Clip) {
        $line += if ($heard) {
            " matched=$($heard['matched']) words=$($heard['words']) errors=$($heard['errors']) stray=$($heard['stray']) split=$($heard['split'])"
        } else {
            ' matched=none'
        }
    }
    [pscustomobject]@{ Name = $Name; Path = $path; Tag = $tag; State = $state; Heard = $heard; Waited = $waited; Passed = $verdict.Passed; Line = $line }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 9: Read the owner's settings and unlock with the test PIN**

In `scripts/lib/Session.ps1`, add at the end of the file:

```powershell

# The owner's settings for the A71 sessions (plan 4D) from corpus\owner\a71-config.json, which git ignores, read as
# JSON, such as {"callNumber": "0641234567", "testContacts": true, "speakerTargetDb": -26, "pin": "..."}: the number
# that is fine to call (5 to 15 digits, with an optional '+'), his consent to the two test contacts, and the level in
# dBFS at which the phone should hear this computer's clips. Every other key, such as those of later plans, comes as
# it is in the file, except the test PIN: this reader, which every part but the unlock uses, leaves out the key "pin",
# which only Send-TestPin reads. A missing file or field gives no number, no consent and -26 dBFS. Throws for a number
# of another form.
function Read-A71Config([string]$Path) {
    $config = [pscustomobject]@{ CallNumber = $null; TestContacts = $false; SpeakerTargetDb = -26.0 }
    if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Path)) { return $config }
    # The parser's own error can repeat the file's text, which holds the test PIN, so it never reaches the console.
    $json = try { Get-Content -LiteralPath $Path -Raw | ConvertFrom-Json } catch { throw "$Path is not valid JSON. Write every value as in {""pin"": ""0246""}, with text in quotes." }
    $field = { param($Name) $json.PSObject.Properties[$Name] }
    if ((& $field 'callNumber') -and "$($json.callNumber)") {
        $number = "$($json.callNumber)" -replace '[\s-]', ''
        if ($number -notmatch '^\+?\d{5,15}$') { throw "$Path`: callNumber must have 5 to 15 digits." }
        $config.CallNumber = $number
    }
    if (& $field 'testContacts') { $config.TestContacts = $json.testContacts -eq $true }
    if ((& $field 'speakerTargetDb') -and $null -ne $json.speakerTargetDb) { $config.SpeakerTargetDb = [double]$json.speakerTargetDb }
    foreach ($property in $json.PSObject.Properties) {
        if ($property.Name -in 'pin', 'callNumber', 'testContacts', 'speakerTargetDb') { continue }
        $config | Add-Member -NotePropertyName $property.Name -NotePropertyValue $property.Value
    }
    $config
}

# Types the A71's test PIN on the phone's PIN pad and presses Enter (plan 4D). The owner allows the A71 sessions to use
# the PIN, which is the key "pin" of $ConfigPath (corpus\owner\a71-config.json), and only this function reads it, right
# before it types it; with -CheckOnly it only checks the key, without a device, as the scripts do at their start. A
# missing file or key, a file that is not JSON, or a PIN that is not 4 to 16 digits in quotes, throws a message that says
# what is wrong and never the PIN. It calls adb itself and never Invoke-SettingsCommand, whose errors repeat the whole command; the device check of
# Format-TestDeviceCommand runs in the same shell call. adb's own output, which can repeat the command too, is dropped,
# the PIN and the command are cleared after use, and the only error of the typing, whatever went wrong, is "the unlock
# failed". Needs scripts\lib\Kestrel.ps1.
function Send-TestPin([string]$Serial, [string]$ConfigPath, [switch]$CheckOnly) {
    $how = 'Write the A71''s test PIN as the key "pin" into corpus\owner\a71-config.json, which git ignores, for example {"pin": "<digits>"}.'
    if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $ConfigPath)) { throw "The A71's test PIN is missing, because $ConfigPath does not exist. $how" }
    $pin = $null
    $command = $null
    $answer = $null
    $sent = $false
    try {
        # The parser's own error can repeat the PIN, as with {"pin": 0246}, so it never reaches the console.
        $json = try { Get-Content -LiteralPath $ConfigPath -Raw | ConvertFrom-Json } catch { throw "$ConfigPath is not valid JSON. Write every value as in {""pin"": ""0246""}, with text in quotes." }
        $value = $json.PSObject.Properties['pin']
        $json = $null
        if (-not $value -or -not "$($value.Value)") { throw "The A71's test PIN is missing, because $ConfigPath has no key ""pin"". $how" }
        # A number, which Windows PowerShell 5.1 reads from {"pin": 0246} as 246, would type another PIN.
        if ($value.Value -isnot [string]) { throw "The key ""pin"" of $ConfigPath must be text in quotes, such as {""pin"": ""0246""}." }
        $pin = "$($value.Value)"
        $value = $null
        if ($pin -notmatch '^\d{4,16}$') { throw "The key ""pin"" of $ConfigPath must be 4 to 16 digits." }
        if ($CheckOnly) { return }
        try {
            $command = Format-TestDeviceCommand -Serial $Serial -Command "input text $pin && input keyevent KEYCODE_ENTER && echo kestrel-pin-sent"
            # Windows PowerShell 5.1 would stop at adb's first error line.
            $ErrorActionPreference = 'Continue'
            $answer = @(& (Get-Adb) -s $Serial shell $command 2>&1 | ForEach-Object { "$_" })
            $sent = $LASTEXITCODE -eq 0 -and "$($answer | Select-Object -Last 1)".Trim() -eq 'kestrel-pin-sent'
        } catch {
            $sent = $false
        }
    } finally {
        $pin = $null
        $command = $null
        $answer = $null
    }
    if (-not $sent) { throw 'the unlock failed' }
}
# Whether an activity shows over the lock screen ('Occluded=true' in 'dumpsys activity activities'), such as a call's
# screen or the camera, which would get what is typed.
function Test-KeyguardOccluded([string[]]$Dump) { @($Dump | Where-Object { $_ -match '(?i)\boccluded=true\b' }).Count -gt 0 }

# Whether the lock screen's PIN pad shows on $Serial: $true only when 'dumpsys activity activities' says that the keyguard
# shows and that no activity shows over it, and the screen's hierarchy holds the PIN pad's entry (the view IDs pinEntry or
# keyguard_pin_view of com.android.systemui); $false when the keyguard does not show; $null when Android does not say it
# clearly. No dumpsys of Android 13 or 17 tells the PIN pad from the rest of the lock screen (both are SystemUI's
# notification shade window), so this reads the hierarchy, once per call: 'uiautomator dump' writes it to
# /data/local/tmp, which the shell owns and which exists before the first unlock after a restart (unlike /sdcard), and
# only the count of those two IDs is read on the phone before the file is deleted, because the hierarchy can hold the
# lock screen's notifications; nothing of it reaches this computer. Each dump unbinds every accessibility service,
# Kestrel's included, for 1 to 10 s, so Kestrel's notice that its service is off can show meanwhile. Needs
# scripts\lib\Kestrel.ps1 and scripts\lib\Oracle.ps1.
function Get-PinPadState([string]$Serial) {
    $activities = @(Invoke-SettingsCommand -Serial $Serial -Command 'dumpsys activity activities')
    $keyguard = Test-KeyguardShowing $activities
    if ($keyguard -ne $true) { return $keyguard }
    if (Test-KeyguardOccluded $activities) { return $null }
    $file = '/data/local/tmp/kestrel-keyguard.xml'
    $count = "$(@(Invoke-SettingsCommand -Serial $Serial -Command "uiautomator dump $file >/dev/null 2>&1; grep -c -e id/pinEntry -e id/keyguard_pin_view $file; rm -f $file") | Select-Object -Last 1)".Trim()
    if ($count -match '^\d+$' -and [int]$count -gt 0) { return $true }
    $null
}

# Unlocks the test phone $Serial with the owner's test PIN of $ConfigPath (Send-TestPin), also right after a restart,
# when Android asks for the PIN: it wakes the screen and, when the keyguard shows and no activity shows over it, brings
# up the PIN pad without looking (a swipe up from the screen's lower part, which Android's lock screen and One UI's both
# answer with the PIN pad, and 'wm dismiss-keyguard', which shows it for a lock screen with a PIN), and then types the PIN
# only when Get-PinPadState says clearly that the PIN pad shows; a state that Android does not say clearly never types, so
# the PIN never reaches another app's field. Returns $true when the phone is unlocked, and $false when it typed nothing
# (the phone did not show its PIN pad). The PIN is typed at most once in a call, and a typing that did not unlock the
# phone within 10 s throws "the unlock failed" and stops every later unlock of the run, which then throws at once: One UI
# locks the phone for a while after 5 wrong PINs, and can reset it after 15 when that is switched on. The PIN never
# reaches a result, a log or the console. Needs scripts\lib\Kestrel.ps1 and scripts\lib\Oracle.ps1.
function Unlock-TestPhone([string]$Serial, [string]$ConfigPath) {
    # A missing file or key stops here, before a command reaches the phone.
    Send-TestPin -ConfigPath $ConfigPath -CheckOnly
    if ($script:TestPinRefused) { throw 'the unlock failed earlier in this run, so the session tries no more unlocks' }
    Invoke-SettingsCommand -Serial $Serial -Command 'input keyevent KEYCODE_WAKEUP' | Out-Null
    Start-Sleep -Seconds 1
    $activities = @(Invoke-SettingsCommand -Serial $Serial -Command 'dumpsys activity activities')
    $keyguard = Test-KeyguardShowing $activities
    if ($keyguard -eq $false) { return $true }
    if ($null -eq $keyguard -or (Test-KeyguardOccluded $activities)) { return $false }
    if ("$(Invoke-SettingsCommand -Serial $Serial -Command 'wm size')" -match '(\d+)x(\d+)') {
        $x = [int]([int]$Matches[1] / 2)
        Invoke-SettingsCommand -Serial $Serial -Command "input swipe $x $([int]([int]$Matches[2] * 0.8)) $x $([int]([int]$Matches[2] * 0.3)) 300" | Out-Null
    }
    Invoke-SettingsCommand -Serial $Serial -Command 'wm dismiss-keyguard' | Out-Null
    Start-Sleep -Seconds 2
    $pad = Get-PinPadState -Serial $Serial
    if ($pad -eq $false) { return $true }
    # Only on the PIN pad: the PIN must never reach another app's field.
    if ($pad -ne $true) { return $false }
    # Set before the typing, so that also a typing that failed half-way is never repeated.
    $script:TestPinRefused = $true
    Send-TestPin -Serial $Serial -ConfigPath $ConfigPath
    for ($second = 0; $second -lt 10; $second++) {
        Start-Sleep -Seconds 1
        if ((Test-KeyguardShowing @(Invoke-SettingsCommand -Serial $Serial -Command 'dumpsys activity activities')) -eq $false) {
            $script:TestPinRefused = $false
            return $true
        }
    }
    throw 'the unlock failed'
}
```

---

### Task 7: The check corpus, its clips and the room-sound track

**Files:**
- Create: `corpus/checks.txt`, `corpus/room-sound.txt`, `scripts/make-room-sound.ps1`
- Modify: `scripts/make-corpus.ps1` (`-List`)
- Test: `scripts/tests/Corpus.Tests.ps1`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 6's `Read-WavFile` and `Write-WavFile`; `scripts\lib\Corpus.ps1`'s `Read-Corpus`; the hook `voice-synth`.
- Produces: `corpus\checks.txt` (ids `k001` to `k023`, in `commands.txt`'s format); `scripts\make-corpus.ps1 -Serial <s> -Voice piper|kokoro -List commands|checks`, whose clips of either list go into `corpus\generated\<voice>\<id>.wav`; `scripts\make-room-sound.ps1 [-Minutes 30]`, which writes `corpus\generated\room-sound.wav` (16 kHz, 16-bit, mono). Both folders are in `corpus\generated`, which git ignores.

The air path plays a clip for each check, and map part 2.3 lists the phrases that `corpus\commands.txt` lacks. `corpus\checks.txt` holds them in the same format, with ids that start with `k`, so that their clips share `corpus\generated\<voice>` with the corpus's clips, and no line is marked for the owner, so `voice-eval.ps1 -Corpus owner` and the corpus's counts do not change. Four phrases of the map get no clip: "call 1234", "tap call" and "call Ana Test", because the air path never runs the call checks (map part 1), and "long press <label>", because its label stays on the phone (`screen-has -Template`, interfaces section 5); "increment N" and "decrement N" get a clip that `voice-synth` makes during the run, because N is the slider's overlay number on the day. Three phrases are new: "Open Calculator.", "Open Clock." and "Open Calendar.", for the map's C55 to C57, which use Samsung's own apps instead of the owner's. The answers "1", "2" and "no" are sent as text also in the air pass, as the runner's `-Answer` does, so "1" gets no clip.

The room sound for battery measure 3 is the map's second choice (part 7.4): Windows' own voices through SAPI read about 70 everyday sentences that this plan wrote (`corpus\room-sound.txt`), shuffled, with a random voice and speed for each and pauses of 0.5 to 3 s, until the track is 30 minutes long. The voices differ from Kestrel's own, the text needs no download and no licence, and a Pester test checks that no sentence holds a word of Kestrel's commands, answers, numbers or names, so the talk can start nothing on the phone (the map's first choice, a LibriVox play, cannot be screened). The script needs no device and runs in both shells.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests of the two corpora**

In `scripts/tests/Corpus.Tests.ps1`, add at the end of the file:

```powershell

Describe 'The corpora of the A71 sessions'' automatic checks' {
    BeforeAll {
        . "$PSScriptRoot\..\lib\Corpus.ps1"
        $root = "$PSScriptRoot\..\..\corpus"
    }

    It 'reads the check corpus, whose ids and phrases are not in corpus\commands.txt' {
        $commands = @(Read-Corpus -Path "$root\commands.txt")
        $checks = @(Read-Corpus -Path "$root\checks.txt")

        $checks.Count | Should -Be 23
        @($checks | Where-Object { $_.Id -notmatch '^k\d{3}$' }).Count | Should -Be 0
        @($checks | Where-Object { $_.Owner }).Count | Should -Be 0
        $known = @($commands | ForEach-Object { $_.Text.ToLowerInvariant() })
        @($checks | Where-Object { $_.Text.ToLowerInvariant() -in $known }).Text | Should -BeNullOrEmpty
    }

    It 'has room sound in which no word of Kestrel''s commands, answers, numbers or names appears' {
        $words = 'kestrel', 'hey', 'call', 'dial', 'phone', 'send', 'sent', 'message', 'text', 'delete', 'emergency', 'sos',
            'open', 'close', 'tap', 'click', 'press', 'scroll', 'swipe', 'back', 'home', 'stop', 'cancel', 'turn', 'volume',
            'mute', 'unmute', 'silence', 'lock', 'power', 'screenshot', 'select', 'copy', 'paste', 'undo', 'redo', 'type', 'show',
            'hide', 'number', 'numbers', 'zoom', 'magnify', 'pan', 'search', 'play', 'pause', 'next', 'previous', 'wake', 'listen',
            'settings', 'notifications', 'wifi', 'bluetooth', 'flashlight', 'camera', 'clock', 'yes', 'no', 'go', 'up', 'down',
            'left', 'right', 'help', 'again', 'line', 'enter', 'capital', 'all', 'end', 'start', 'top', 'bottom', 'edge', 'grid',
            'labels', 'app', 'apps', 'recent', 'quick', 'save', 'done', 'ok', 'okay', 'read', 'wait', 'never', 'mind', 'forget',
            'nothing', 'first', 'second', 'third', 'hello', 'alarm', 'music', 'media', 'display', 'keyboard', 'password'
        $numbers = 'zero', 'one', 'two', 'three', 'four', 'five', 'six', 'seven', 'eight', 'nine', 'ten', 'eleven', 'twelve',
            'twenty', 'hundred', 'thousand'
        $sentences = @([IO.File]::ReadAllLines("$root\room-sound.txt", [Text.UTF8Encoding]::new($false)) |
                Where-Object { $_.Trim() -and -not $_.TrimStart().StartsWith('#') })

        $sentences.Count | Should -BeGreaterThan 60
        $found = foreach ($sentence in $sentences) {
            foreach ($word in @($sentence.ToLowerInvariant() -split '[^a-z0-9]+' | Where-Object { $_ })) {
                if ($word -in $words -or $word -in $numbers -or $word -match '\d') { $word }
            }
        }
        @($found) | Should -BeNullOrEmpty
    }
}
```

# The phrases of the A71 sessions' automatic checks that corpus\commands.txt lacks (plan 4D), in its format: the air
# path of scripts\a71-commands.ps1 and scripts\a71-session.ps1 plays their clips through this computer's speakers, and
# the phone matches each one to its text (hook voice-expect). scripts\make-corpus.ps1 -List checks says each line in one
# of Kestrel's voices into corpus\generated\<voice>, next to the clips of commands.txt, so the ids start with k. No line
